# List 23

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sermon</b></td><td>/ˈsɜːrmən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>布道，说教</td><td>deliver a sermon<br>进行一场布道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old priest delivered a sermon about forgiveness that lasted an hour.  老神父做了一场关于宽恕的布道，持续了一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homily</td><td>/ˈhɑːməli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冗长乏味的道德讲演或训诫</td><td>deliver a homily<br>作一番训诫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had to sit through another homily about punctuality and respect.  我们只得又听了一通关于守时与尊重的冗长训诫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>serpentine</b></td><td>/ˈsɜːrpəntiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的</td><td>a serpentine mountain road<br>蜿蜒的山路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We drove slowly along a serpentine mountain road above the valley.  我们沿着山谷上方蜿蜒的山路缓慢前行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curved</td><td>/ˈkɜːrvd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的，弧形的</td><td>a gently curved line<br>轻微弯曲的线条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge has a curved deck of pale stone.  这座桥有一段淡色石头的弧形桥面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curvy</td><td>/ˈkɜːrvi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多曲线的，弯曲的</td><td>a curvy coastal road<br>弯弯曲曲的海滨公路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We drove slowly along the curvy road above the sea.  我们沿海边弯曲的公路慢慢开车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sinuous</td><td>/ˈsɪnjuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>蜿蜒的，迂回的</td><td>a sinuous river<br>蜿蜒的河流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sinuous river winds through the wide valley below the village.  一条蜿蜒的河流穿过村子下方宽阔的谷地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tortuous</td><td>/ˈtɔːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>转弯抹角的</td><td>a tortuous argument<br>转弯抹角的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tortuous explanation left the whole committee more confused than before.  他转弯抹角的解释让整个委员会比之前更困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twisted</td><td>/ˈtwɪstɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扭曲的；反常的</td><td>a twisted plot<br>曲折离奇的情节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's twisted plot keeps readers guessing until the last page.  这部小说曲折的情节让读者猜到最后一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winding</td><td>/ˈwaɪndɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>蜿蜒的，曲折的</td><td>a winding staircase<br>蜿蜒的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A winding path led up to the old chapel.  一条蜿蜒的小路通向老教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 direct</td><td>/dəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直接的，径直的</td><td>a direct flight<br>直飞航班</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ask for a direct answer instead of a long explanation.  要个直接的答复，而不是长篇解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straight</td><td>/ˈstreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直的，笔直的</td><td>a straight road<br>笔直的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drive straight ahead until you reach the second traffic light.  一直往前开，直到第二个红绿灯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straightaway</td><td>/ˈstreɪtəˌweɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>笔直的；直道</td><td>a long straightaway stretch<br>长长的笔直路段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cars reach top speed on the final straightaway.  赛车在最后的直道上达到最高速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>selfdefeating</b></del></td><td>/ˌselfdɪˈfiːtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适得其反的，自我拆台的</td><td>a selfdefeating strategy<br>自我拆台的策略</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the research budget is a selfdefeating strategy that will harm future profits.  削减研究预算是一种适得其反的策略，会损害未来的利润。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>serried</b></td><td>/ˈserid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>密集的</td><td>serried ranks of soldiers<br>密集的士兵队列</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Serried ranks of soldiers filled the square before the parade began.  阅兵开始前，密集的士兵队列填满了广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compact</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>契约，协议；紧凑的</td><td>sign a compact<br>签订协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a compact to protect the shared river.  两国签订协议保护共有的河流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dense</td><td>/ˈdens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>密集的，稠密的</td><td>a dense forest<br>茂密的森林</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dense fog closed the airport for three hours.  浓雾使机场关闭了三小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 widely separated</td><td>/ˈwaɪdli ˈsɛpəreɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相距很远的，稀疏的</td><td>widely separated houses<br>相距很远的房屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farms lay widely separated across the dry plain.  农场在干旱平原上彼此相距很远。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 airy</td><td>/ˈeri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽敞通风的</td><td>a light airy room<br>明亮通风的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rented an airy apartment with tall windows.  他们租了一间有高窗、通风良好的公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 open</td><td>/ˈoʊpən/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>公开的，坦率的</td><td>open opposition<br>公开的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open opposition to the plan at the meeting.  会上有人公开反对这项计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncrowded</td><td>/ʌnˈkraʊdɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不拥挤的，宽松的</td><td>an uncrowded beach<br>不拥挤的海滩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We chose an uncrowded train in the early afternoon.  我们选了午后一班不拥挤的火车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>servile</b></td><td>/ˈsɜːrvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑屈的，奴性的</td><td>servile flattery<br>奴性的谄媚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His servile manner toward the boss annoyed everyone.  他对老板卑躬屈膝的态度让大家反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 menial</td><td>/ˈmiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的，低下的</td><td>menial tasks<br>低贱的杂活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took menial jobs to pay for night school.  她做低微的工作来支付夜校学费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slavish</td><td>/ˈsleɪvɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>盲从的，奴性的</td><td>slavish imitation<br>盲目的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The design is a slavish imitation of an older model.  这个设计是对旧款的盲目模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shifty</b></td><td>/ˈʃɪfti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（显得）狡诈的，（显得）欺诈的</td><td>shifty eyes<br>游移闪躲的眼神</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman had shifty eyes that made every customer uneasy.  那推销员眼神游移闪躲，让每个顾客都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crooked</td><td>/ˈkrʊkəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的；歪的；不诚实的</td><td>a crooked country road<br>弯曲的乡间小路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old painting hung crooked above the fireplace for years.  那幅旧画歪歪地挂在壁炉上方多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceitful</td><td>/dəˈsiːtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的，骗人的</td><td>deceitful practices<br>欺诈的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm was fined for deceitful advertising practices.  该公司因欺诈性广告做法被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceptive</td><td>/dɪˈseptɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺骗性的，误导的</td><td>deceptive advertising<br>欺骗性广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The calm surface of the river is deceptive and dangerous.  河面的平静具有欺骗性，很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duplicitous</td><td>/duːˈplɪsɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>两面派的，奸诈的</td><td>duplicitous behavior<br>两面派的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His duplicitous behavior toward both partners eventually destroyed the company.  他对两位合伙人的两面派行为最终毁了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraudulent</td><td>/ˈfrɔːdʒələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的</td><td>fraudulent claims<br>欺诈性的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The insurer refused to pay several obviously fraudulent claims last year.  去年保险公司拒付了几笔明显欺诈的索赔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 guileful</del></td><td>/ˈɡaɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，欺骗的</td><td>a guileful negotiator<br>狡诈的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guileful merchant tricked several villagers out of their savings.  这个狡诈的商人骗走了几位村民的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rogue</td><td>/ˈroʊɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓</td><td>a charming rogue<br>讨人喜欢的无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Audiences forgive the hero because he is a charming rogue.  观众原谅这位主角，因为他是个迷人的无赖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shady</td><td>/ˈʃeɪdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可疑的，不正当的</td><td>a shady business deal<br>可疑的商业交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His money came from several shady arrangements abroad.  他的钱来自海外几笔可疑的安排。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underhanded</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈhændɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，暗中进行的</td><td>underhanded tactics<br>暗中使用的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They won the contract through underhanded tactics.  他们靠不正当手段赢得了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aboveboard</td><td>/əˈbʌvbɔːrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无欺诈的，光明正大的</td><td>open and aboveboard<br>公开而光明正大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole deal was entirely open and aboveboard.  整笔交易完全是光明正大的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straight</td><td>/ˈstreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直的，笔直的</td><td>a straight road<br>笔直的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drive straight ahead until you reach the second traffic light.  一直往前开，直到第二个红绿灯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shackle</b></td><td>/ˈʃækl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>束缚；限制，阻碍</td><td>shackle free trade<br>束缚自由贸易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New tariffs would shackle trade between the two neighboring countries.  新关税会束缚这两个邻国之间的贸易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chain</td><td>/ˈtʃeɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>连接，用链系住；链条</td><td>chain the parts together<br>把各部分连起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He chained his bicycle to the railing outside the shop.  他把自行车锁在店外的栏杆上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enchain</del></td><td>/ɛnˈtʃeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用链锁住，束缚</td><td>enchain the prisoners<br>用锁链锁住囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old habits can enchain a person more tightly than any rope.  旧习惯对人的束缚比任何绳索都紧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enfetter</del></td><td>/ɛnˈfɛtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>束缚，使受奴役</td><td>enfetter the human spirit<br>束缚人的精神</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old superstitions still enfetter the minds of many villagers.  旧迷信仍束缚着许多村民的思想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetter</td><td>/ˈfetər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚</td><td>the fetters of tradition<br>传统的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy family obligations fettered her ambition for many long years.  沉重的家庭责任多年来束缚了她的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gyve</del></td><td>/dʒaɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>脚镣，镣铐</td><td>bound in gyves<br>被镣铐锁住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner shuffled forward in heavy gyves.  囚犯戴着沉重脚镣蹭步向前。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handcuff</td><td>/ˈhændˌkʌf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>束缚；给戴手铐</td><td>handcuff the suspect<br>给嫌疑人戴上手铐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officers handcuffed the man before leading him away.  警官在带走这名男子前给他戴上手铐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 manacle</del></td><td>/ˈmænəkl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给…戴上手铐；束缚，限制</td><td>manacle the prisoner<br>给囚犯戴上手铐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy debts manacle the young couple, leaving them no freedom to change careers.  沉重的债务束缚了这对年轻夫妇，让他们没有转行的自由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pinion</td><td>/ˈpɪnjən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>束缚，捆住</td><td>pinion someone's arms<br>把某人的手臂捆住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two guards pinioned his arms behind his back.  两名警卫把他的手臂反捆在背后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trammel</td><td>/ˈtræməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚，限制</td><td>the trammels of custom<br>习俗的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old rules trammel the creativity of young designers here.  旧规矩束缚了这里年轻设计师的创造力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clog</td><td>/klɑːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>阻碍物</td><td>a clog on progress<br>进步的障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old regulations act as a clog on economic progress.  陈旧的法规成了经济进步的阻碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cramp</td><td>/ˈkræmp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>限制，束缚；痉挛</td><td>cramp his style<br>束缚他的发挥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of money cramped every plan the young director had.  资金短缺束缚了这位年轻导演的每个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encumber</td><td>/ɪnˈkʌmbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍；妨碍</td><td>encumber the project<br>妨碍这个项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many rules encumber small businesses in this country.  太多规定阻碍了这个国家的小企业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impede</td><td>/ɪmˈpiːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻碍</td><td>impede progress<br>阻碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick fog impeded the rescue team's progress.  浓雾阻碍了救援队的前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhibit</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，阻碍</td><td>inhibit the growth of bacteria<br>抑制细菌生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold weather inhibits the growth of these tropical plants.  寒冷天气抑制了这些热带植物的生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obstruct</td><td>/əbˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻塞</td><td>obstruct justice<br>妨碍司法公正</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fallen tree obstructed the only road out of town.  一棵倒下的树堵住了唯一的出城道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 short-circuit</td><td>/ˌʃɔːrt ˈsɜːrkɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使中断，绕过</td><td>short-circuit the process<br>绕过正常流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tried to short-circuit the review by calling the director.  他们打电话给主管，想绕过审查流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stymie</td><td>/ˈstaɪmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>stymie an investigation<br>阻碍调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Missing records stymied the investigation for months.  档案缺失使调查停滞了数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tie up</td><td>/taɪ ʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>捆住；使受阻，占住</td><td>tie up the traffic<br>使交通受阻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The accident tied up traffic for more than two hours.  事故使交通堵塞了两个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emancipate</td><td>/ɪˈmænsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，解除束缚</td><td>emancipate the slaves<br>解放奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new law emancipated the slaves working on the northern plantations.  新法律解放了在北方种植园劳作的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unbind</td><td>/ʌnˈbaɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，松开，释放</td><td>unbind the prisoner<br>解开囚犯的绑缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She knelt down to unbind the wounded man's hands.  她跪下来解开那名伤者被绑的双手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unfetter</del></td><td>/ʌnˈfɛtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解除束缚，使自由</td><td>unfetter the market<br>解除对市场的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform unfettered small firms and let them compete freely.  改革解除了对小企业的束缚，让它们自由竞争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unshackle</td><td>/ʌnˈʃækəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开束缚，释放</td><td>unshackle the economy<br>解除对经济的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reforms unshackled small businesses from needless paperwork.  改革让小企业摆脱了无谓的文书负担。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aid</td><td>/ˈeɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>帮助，援助</td><td>come to someone's aid<br>前来帮助某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors came to her aid within minutes of the fire.  火灾发生几分钟内邻居们就来帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assist</td><td>/əˈsɪst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>帮助，协助</td><td>assist with the work<br>协助工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two nurses assisted the surgeon during the operation.  两名护士在手术中协助医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facilitate</td><td>/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变容易，促进</td><td>facilitate the discussion<br>促进讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new airport will facilitate tourism throughout the whole region.  新机场将促进整个地区的旅游业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 help</td><td>/ˈhelp/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>帮助，助益</td><td>a great help<br>很大的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your advice was a great help during the difficult week.  在那困难的一周里你的建议帮了大忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shadow</b></td><td>/ˈʃædoʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偷偷尾随</td><td>shadow a suspect<br>偷偷尾随嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two detectives agreed to shadow the suspect through the crowded market.  两名侦探决定在拥挤的市场里偷偷尾随嫌疑人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chase</td><td>/tʃeɪs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>驱赶</td><td>chase away<br>赶走，驱散</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer chased the crows away from his young corn.  农夫把乌鸦从他的嫩玉米地里赶走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 course</td><td>/ˈkɔːrs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>奔流，快速行进</td><td>course through his veins<br>在他血管中奔流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Adrenaline coursed through his veins as the race finally began.  比赛终于开始时肾上腺素在他血管中奔涌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pursue</td><td>/pərˈsuː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>追求，追寻；追捕</td><td>pursue a career<br>追求事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She pursued him for months before he finally agreed to dinner.  她追了他好几个月，他才终于答应共进晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trace</td><td>/ˈtreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痕迹；微量</td><td>a trace of irony<br>一丝讽意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tea showed only a trace of bitterness.  这茶只有一丝苦味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 track</td><td>/ˈtræk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>跟踪，追踪</td><td>track a moving target<br>追踪移动目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rangers tracked the wounded bear for two days.  护林员追踪那头受伤的熊两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trail</td><td>/ˈtreɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>跟踪；拖曳</td><td>trail someone closely<br>紧紧跟踪某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gray car trailed them all the way home.  一辆灰色汽车一路跟踪他们回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>stale</b></td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moth-eaten</td><td>/ˈmɔːθ ˌiːtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虫蛀的；陈旧过时的</td><td>a moth-eaten coat<br>被虫蛀的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a moth-eaten sweater from his college days.  他穿着大学时代那件被虫蛀的毛衣来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 musty</td><td>/ˈmʌsti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发霉的，霉味的；陈腐的</td><td>a musty smell<br>一股霉味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cellar was dark, damp, and full of musty old books.  地窖阴暗潮湿，堆满带霉味的旧书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shopworn</td><td>/ˈʃɑːpwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，老一套的</td><td>shopworn arguments<br>陈腐的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate repeated shopworn arguments that convinced almost nobody in the hall.  那位候选人重复着陈腐的论点，几乎没能说服场内任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stereotyped</td><td>/ˈsteriətaɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>老套的，刻板的</td><td>stereotyped characters<br>刻板的人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of stereotyped characters and predictable plots.  这部小说充满刻板人物和可预测的情节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 timeworn</td><td>/ˈtaɪmˌwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，老套的</td><td>a timeworn saying<br>老掉牙的谚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He comforted us with timeworn advice about patience and hard work.  他用关于耐心与勤奋的老套建议安慰我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 new</td><td>/ˈnuː/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新的，最近出现的</td><td>a brand new car<br>一辆全新的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a new apartment near the river.  他们搬进了河边一套新公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novel</td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unhackneyed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhæknid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新颖的</td><td>an unhackneyed phrase<br>一个新颖的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essay pleased the judges with its fresh, unhackneyed images.  她的文章以新鲜不俗的意象取悦了评委。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sham</b></td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 act</td><td>/ˈækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>装样子，表演；行动</td><td>act for the crowd<br>为观众做样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was only acting hurt to win the crowd's sympathy.  他只是装作受伤，以博取观众同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affect</td><td>/əˈfekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>假装，装作；影响</td><td>affect indifference<br>装作漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She affected surprise, though she had known for weeks.  她装作惊讶，其实早已知情数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluff</td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterfeit</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərfɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>仿制的，假冒的；仿制</td><td>counterfeit banknotes<br>伪造的钞票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police seized thousands of counterfeit banknotes hidden in the delivery van.  警方缴获了藏在送货车里的数千张假钞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cloak</td><td>/kloʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遮掩，隐藏</td><td>cloak the truth<br>掩盖真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials cloaked the truth behind pages of confusing statistics.  官员用一堆令人困惑的统计数字掩盖真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissemble</td><td>/dɪˈsembl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>用假象隐藏真相，掩饰</td><td>dissemble his true motive<br>掩饰他的真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to dissemble his true motive, but his trembling hands betrayed him.  他试图掩饰真实动机，但颤抖的双手泄露了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simulate</td><td>/ˈsɪmjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模拟，假装</td><td>simulate battle conditions<br>模拟作战环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The training program can simulate battle conditions inside a quiet classroom.  该训练项目能在安静的教室里模拟作战环境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretend</td><td>/prɪˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>假装，装作</td><td>pretend to be asleep<br>装睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pretended not to hear their mother calling.  孩子们假装没听见母亲呼唤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profess</td><td>/prəˈfes/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称，假称</td><td>profess ignorance<br>声称不知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He professed great sympathy but offered no actual help.  他声称非常同情，却没提供实际帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put on</td><td>/pʊt ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>穿上，戴上</td><td>put on a jacket<br>穿上夹克</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She put on her gloves before stepping into the snow.  她走进雪地前戴上了手套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pass for</td><td>/ˈpæs fɔːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>被当作，冒充</td><td>pass for an expert<br>冒充专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With that uniform he could pass for a guard.  穿着那身制服他可以冒充警卫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bogus</td><td>/ˈboʊɡəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伪造的，假冒的</td><td>a bogus claim<br>虚假的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police discovered a stack of bogus passports in the van.  警方在货车里发现一叠伪造护照。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forged</td><td>/ˈfɔːrdʒd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伪造的，假冒的</td><td>a forged signature<br>伪造的签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank noticed a forged signature on the withdrawal slip.  银行发现取款单上有伪造的签名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spurious</td><td>/ˈspjʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，伪造的</td><td>spurious claims<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The website spread spurious claims about the new vaccine.  该网站传播关于新疫苗的虚假说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unauthentic</td><td>/ˌʌnɔˈθentɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不真实的，非原作的</td><td>an unauthentic signature<br>一个不真实的签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts declared the painting unauthentic after careful chemical testing.  专家经仔细化学检测后宣布这幅画并非真品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 authentic</td><td>/ɔːˈθentɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，非仿造的</td><td>authentic Italian cuisine<br>正宗的意大利菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum confirmed the letter was an authentic original.  博物馆确认这封信是真实的原件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bona fide</td><td>/ˌboʊnə ˈfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，真实的，善意的</td><td>a bona fide offer<br>真诚的报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only bona fide students may borrow books from this library.  只有真正的在校学生才能从本馆借书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 real</td><td>/ˈriːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，真正的</td><td>a real event<br>真实事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is based on a real event from 1943.  这部电影基于1943年的一件真实事件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unfaked</del></td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪkt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，未伪造的</td><td>unfaked emotion<br>真实未做作的情感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her surprise was clearly unfaked when the door opened.  门打开时她的惊讶显然不是假装的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shiftless</b></td><td>/ˈʃɪftləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胸无大志的，懒惰的</td><td>a shiftless drifter<br>游手好闲的流浪者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His father dismissed him as a shiftless young man without ambition.  他父亲斥他为没有志向、游手好闲的年轻人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idle</td><td>/ˈaɪdl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲置的，未使用的；懒散的</td><td>idle machines<br>闲置的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Half the factory's machines stood idle for most of last year.  这家工厂一半的机器去年大部分时间都闲置着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indolent</td><td>/ˈɪndələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的</td><td>an indolent young man<br>懒散的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too indolent to look for work, he simply lay on the couch all summer.  他懒得去找工作，整个夏天就躺在沙发上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slothful</td><td>/ˈsloʊθfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的，迟缓的</td><td>slothful habits<br>懒散的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of easy living had made the once-diligent clerk slothful.  多年安逸生活使这位曾经勤勉的职员变得懒散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 industrious</td><td>/ɪnˈdʌstriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，刻苦的</td><td>an industrious worker<br>勤劳的工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most industrious student in the class reviewed her notes every single night.  班里最勤奋的学生每晚都复习笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shipshape</b></td><td>/ˈʃɪpʃeɪp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的</td><td>keep the office shipshape<br>让办公室井然有序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor kept his tiny cabin perfectly shipshape at all times.  老水手始终把他的小舱室保持得井然有序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antiseptic</td><td>/ˌæntəˈseptɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>洁净无菌的；防腐剂</td><td>an antiseptic white room<br>洁净无菌的白色房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab was antiseptic and almost uncomfortably tidy.  实验室洁净得几乎让人不自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kempt</td><td>/kɛmpt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，梳理整齐的</td><td>a kempt appearance<br>整洁的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived kempt and calm despite the long flight.  尽管长途飞行，他到达时仍整洁镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prim</td><td>/ˈprɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，一本正经的</td><td>prim and proper<br>一本正经</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sat very prim on the edge of the borrowed chair.  她一本正经地坐在借来的椅子边上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncluttered</td><td>/ʌnˈklʌtərd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>整齐不杂乱的</td><td>an uncluttered desk<br>不杂乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She works best at an uncluttered table.  她在不杂乱的桌前工作效率最高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-groomed</td><td>/ˌwɛl ˈɡruːmd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a well-groomed candidate<br>衣冠整洁的候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The well-groomed clerk greeted every visitor politely.  这位衣冠整洁的职员礼貌地招待每位来客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disheveled</td><td>/dɪˈʃevəld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，蓬乱的</td><td>disheveled hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked disheveled after sleeping in his clothes.  他穿着衣服睡过之后显得很凌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disordered</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，无秩序的</td><td>a disordered pile of papers<br>杂乱的一堆文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disordered shelves made finding anything impossible.  杂乱的书架让人无法找到任何东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disorderly</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，无秩序的</td><td>a disorderly crowd<br>混乱的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Books lay in disorderly piles across the whole floor.  书杂乱地堆在整个地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 messy</td><td>/ˈmesi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，脏乱的</td><td>a messy bedroom<br>凌乱的卧室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His room was so messy that the floor disappeared.  他房间乱得连地板都看不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 sloven</del></td><td>/ˈslʌvən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不整洁的人，懒散者</td><td>live like a sloven<br>像懒散者一样生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a sloven would leave dishes for a week.  只有懒散的人才会把碗放一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unkempt</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkempt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，蓬乱的</td><td>unkempt hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the interview with unkempt hair and a wrinkled shirt.  他去面试时头发蓬乱，衬衫也皱皱巴巴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untidy</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，凌乱的</td><td>an untidy desk<br>凌乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His untidy desk was buried under unopened letters.  他凌乱的书桌被未开封的信件埋住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shirk</b></td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avoid</td><td>/əˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，回避</td><td>avoid the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully avoided answering the reporter's most difficult question.  他小心地避开了记者最难的那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elude</td><td>/ɪˈluːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲闪，躲避；使无法理解，使困惑</td><td>elude the police<br>躲开警察</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief eluded the police for nearly six months before being caught.  小偷躲开警方近六个月才被抓住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eschew</td><td>/ɪsˈtʃuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意避开；戒绝</td><td>eschew violence<br>戒绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks eschew luxury and live on whatever the village offers.  僧侣们避开奢华，靠村民所给的一切生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evade</td><td>/ɪˈveɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>evade the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness evaded every question about the missing money.  证人回避了所有关于失踪钱款的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shun</td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weasel out of</td><td>/ˈwiːzəl aʊt ʌv/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>推脱，逃避责任</td><td>weasel out of a promise<br>逃避承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to weasel out of the contract by citing vague technicalities.  他试图以含糊的技术细节为由逃避合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shoal</b></td><td>/ʃoʊl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅的</td><td>shoal water ahead<br>前方水浅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain warned us about the shoal water near the reef.  船长提醒我们注意礁石附近的浅水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deep</td><td>/ˈdiːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深刻的，深奥的</td><td>a deep analysis<br>深刻的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay offers a deep reading of the novel's ending.  这篇文章对小说结尾作了深刻解读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shopworn</b></td><td>/ˈʃɑːpwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，老一套的</td><td>shopworn arguments<br>陈腐的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate repeated shopworn arguments that convinced almost nobody in the hall.  那位候选人重复着陈腐的论点，几乎没能说服场内任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-worn</td><td>/wel ˈwɔːrn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；用旧的</td><td>a well-worn phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker repeated a well-worn joke about airline food.  演讲者又讲了个关于飞机餐的老掉牙笑话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 new</td><td>/ˈnuː/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新的，最近出现的</td><td>a brand new car<br>一辆全新的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a new apartment near the river.  他们搬进了河边一套新公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novel</td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unclichéd</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkliːˈʃeɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新鲜的</td><td>an unclichéd ending<br>不落俗套的结尾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the film for its unclichéd dialogue.  评论家称赞这部电影不落俗套的对话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unhackneyed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhæknid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新颖的</td><td>an unhackneyed phrase<br>一个新颖的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essay pleased the judges with its fresh, unhackneyed images.  她的文章以新鲜不俗的意象取悦了评委。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shred</b></td><td>/ʃred/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>碎片，细条；少量</td><td>not a shred of evidence<br>没有一丝证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is not a shred of evidence linking him to the theft.  没有一丝证据能把他和这起盗窃联系起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crumb</td><td>/ˈkrʌm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>碎屑，少量</td><td>not a crumb of evidence<br>一丝证据也没有</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swept every crumb from the table with his hand.  他用手把桌上每一点碎屑都扫掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 driblet</del></td><td>/ˈdrɪblət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，点滴</td><td>in driblets<br>一点一点地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 News of the accident arrived in driblets all evening.  事故的消息整晚一点一点地传来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mite</td><td>/maɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微小的东西，很少的钱</td><td>a mite of comfort<br>一点点安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brief letter offered a mite of comfort during those difficult winter months.  他简短的信在那些艰难的冬月里带来了一点点安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scintilla</td><td>/sɪnˈtɪlə/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一丝，极少量</td><td>not a scintilla of doubt<br>毫无一丝怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is not a scintilla of proof for that claim.  那个说法没有一丝证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spark</td><td>/ˈspɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引发，激起</td><td>spark a heated debate<br>引发激烈争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark sparked a long argument at the table.  一句不慎的话在餐桌上引发了长久的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 speck</td><td>/spek/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>小点，少量</td><td>a speck of dust<br>一粒微小的灰尘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single speck of dust on the lens ruined the photograph.  镜头上一粒灰尘毁了这张照片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sprinkling</td><td>/ˈsprɪŋklɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，稀疏的一点</td><td>a sprinkling of snow<br>薄薄一层雪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was only a sprinkling of guests in the hall.  大厅里只有稀稀落落几位客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tad</td><td>/ˈtæd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一点儿，少量</td><td>a tad too salty<br>稍微咸了一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup needs just a tad more pepper.  这汤只需再加一点点胡椒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shrewd</b></td><td>/ʃruːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精明的，机敏的</td><td>a shrewd judgment<br>精明的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a shrewd judgment that saved the company millions of dollars.  那是个精明的判断，为公司省下了数百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crafty</td><td>/ˈkræfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的，诡计多端的</td><td>a crafty politician<br>狡猾的政客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A crafty lawyer found a loophole nobody else had noticed.  一位狡猾的律师找到了别人都没注意的漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cunning</td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a cunning scheme<br>狡猾的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fox was cunning enough to avoid every trap they set.  这只狐狸够狡猾，避开了他们设的每个陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sly</td><td>/slaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a sly grin<br>狡黠的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sly grin spread across his face as he hid the letter.  他藏起那封信时，脸上露出狡黠的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subtle</td><td>/ˈsʌtl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，难以感知的</td><td>a subtle difference<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a subtle difference between these two shades of blue.  这两种蓝色之间有细微的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wily</td><td>/ˈwaɪli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，狡猾的</td><td>a wily negotiator<br>狡猾的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wily negotiator, he never revealed his lowest acceptable price.  作为狡猾的谈判者，他从不透露自己能接受的最低价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astute</td><td>/əˈstuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，有洞察力的</td><td>an astute observer<br>敏锐的观察者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An astute observer would have noticed the tension in the room.  一个机敏的观察者会注意到房间里的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savvy</td><td>/ˈsævi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>老练，机智；有见识的，精明能干的</td><td>political savvy<br>政治上的老练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her political savvy helped the bill pass with almost no opposition.  她政治上的老练使那项议案几乎无异议通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 naive</td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unknowing</td><td>/ʌnˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知情的，无意识的</td><td>an unknowing accomplice<br>不知情的帮凶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was an unknowing carrier of the virus for weeks.  她在数周内一直是不知情的病毒携带者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shrink</b></td><td>/ʃrɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（在数量或价值方面）降低，减小</td><td>shrink in value<br>价值缩水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The value of the old coins began to shrink after the scandal.  丑闻之后，那些旧币的价值开始缩水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compress</td><td>/kəmˈpres/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>压缩，压紧</td><td>compress the file<br>压缩文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You should compress the file before sending it by email.  用邮件发送之前你应该压缩这个文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condense</td><td>/kənˈdens/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>压缩，缩短；使凝结</td><td>condense the article<br>压缩这篇文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She managed to condense three chapters into a single clear page.  她把三章内容压缩成清晰的一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constrict</td><td>/kənsˈtrɪkt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>收缩，压缩；限制</td><td>constrict blood vessels<br>使血管收缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold air constricts the blood vessels near the skin's surface.  冷空气使皮肤表层的血管收缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 balloon</td><td>/bəˈluːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>迅速增加</td><td>costs have ballooned<br>成本迅速膨胀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repair costs ballooned from two thousand to twenty thousand dollars.  维修费用从两千美元猛增到两万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expand</td><td>/ɪkˈspænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，扩张；详述</td><td>expand the business<br>扩大业务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to expand into three new markets.  公司计划扩张到三个新市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 snowball</td><td>/ˈsnoʊˌbɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>迅速增长，滚雪球般扩大</td><td>snowball into a crisis<br>演变成一场危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small complaint snowballed into a national controversy.  一个小小的投诉迅速演变成全国性争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swell</td><td>/ˈswel/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>膨胀，增大</td><td>swell in numbers<br>数量增加</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd swelled to thousands by early evening.  傍晚时人群增加到了数千人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shroud</b></td><td>/ʃraʊd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>将…从视线中隔离，遮蔽</td><td>shrouded in mist<br>笼罩在雾中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick mist shrouded the harbor and hid the ships from view.  浓雾笼罩港口，把船只从视线中遮住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belie</td><td>/bɪˈlaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>与…相对立；与…相矛盾</td><td>belie his claims<br>与他的说法矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's own records belie his claims about the accident.  公司自己的记录与他关于事故的说法相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanket</td><td>/ˈblæŋkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>覆盖以扑灭；毯子</td><td>blanket the flames<br>覆盖扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters blanketed the burning oil with thick white foam.  消防员用厚厚的白色泡沫覆盖扑灭了燃烧的油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cloak</td><td>/kloʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遮掩，隐藏</td><td>cloak the truth<br>掩盖真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials cloaked the truth behind pages of confusing statistics.  官员用一堆令人困惑的统计数字掩盖真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conceal</td><td>/kənˈsiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>隐藏，隐瞒</td><td>conceal the truth<br>隐瞒真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness tried to conceal the truth from the police.  证人试图向警方隐瞒真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cover</td><td>/ˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，遮盖；覆盖物</td><td>cover up the truth<br>掩盖真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tried to cover up the truth with a vague statement.  他们试图用含糊的声明掩盖真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curtain</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>遮蔽，掩盖；窗帘</td><td>curtain off the room<br>用帘子隔开房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy drapes curtained the windows and hid the room from view.  厚重的帷幔遮住窗户，把房间藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disguise</td><td>/dɪsˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装</td><td>disguise his intentions<br>掩饰他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He disguised his disappointment behind a polite and steady smile.  他用礼貌平静的微笑掩饰了失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mask</td><td>/ˈmæsk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，掩饰；面具</td><td>mask his anger<br>掩饰他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud music masked the sound of the approaching helicopter.  响亮的音乐掩盖了直升机靠近的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 screen</td><td>/skriːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>掩护物，屏障；掩护，遮蔽</td><td>a screen of trees<br>一道树木屏障</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A thick screen of trees hid the old house from the road.  一道浓密的树木屏障把老屋从路上遮住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veil</td><td>/ˈveɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>遮掩，掩饰；面纱</td><td>veil his true motives<br>掩饰真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polite words could not veil his true motives for long.  客气话没能长久掩饰他的真实动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blot out</td><td>/blɑːt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>遮蔽，抹去</td><td>blot out the sun<br>遮蔽太阳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick smoke blotted out the entire skyline.  浓烟遮蔽了整条天际线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paper over</td><td>/ˈpeɪpər ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>掩盖，粉饰</td><td>paper over the differences<br>掩盖分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement papered over deep splits in the party.  这份声明掩盖了党内的深刻分裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bare</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>揭露，袒露；赤裸的</td><td>bare the truth<br>揭露真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the interview he bared his family's darkest secrets.  在采访中他揭露了家族最黑暗的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 display</td><td>/dɪsˈpleɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>展示，陈列；显露</td><td>display the paintings<br>展出这些画作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays its rarest coins in a locked glass case.  博物馆把最珍稀的钱币陈列在上锁的玻璃柜里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 divulge</td><td>/daɪˈvʌldʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>泄漏（秘密）</td><td>divulge a secret<br>泄露秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No employee may divulge a secret learned during the merger negotiations.  任何员工都不得泄露在并购谈判中得知的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 show</td><td>/ˈʃoʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>显示，展现；展览</td><td>show your feelings<br>表露感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rarely shows his feelings, even to close friends.  他很少表露感情，连对好友也如此。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shrug</b></td><td>/ʃrʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，忽略</td><td>shrug off criticism<br>对批评不予理会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The authorities simply shrugged off criticism from local residents.  当局对当地居民的批评一笑置之。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bypass</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌpæs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>绕过，避开</td><td>bypass the rules<br>绕开规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new road bypasses the crowded center of the town.  新路绕开了拥挤的镇中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloss</td><td>/ɡlɑːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>简短解释；敷衍潦草做事（甚至忽略）</td><td>gloss over the problem<br>敷衍带过问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor added a short gloss explaining the unfamiliar legal term.  编者加了一条简短的解释，说明这个陌生的法律术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minimize</td><td>/ˈmɪnɪmaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意低估</td><td>minimize the risks<br>刻意淡化风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company tried to minimize the risks in its statement to worried investors.  公司在给焦虑投资者的声明中试图刻意低估风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overpass</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌpæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>忽略，越过</td><td>overpass minor faults<br>忽略小的过失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor overpassed several errors in the final draft.  编辑忽略了终稿中的几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slur</td><td>/slɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说；诋毁，污辱</td><td>slur one's words<br>说话含糊不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so tired that he began to slur his words.  他累得开始说话含糊不清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heed</td><td>/ˈhiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>注意，留心</td><td>heed the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few sailors heeded the warning about the coming storm.  很少有水手留意关于风暴来临的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mind</td><td>/ˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>留意，注意；介意</td><td>mind the step<br>留意台阶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please mind the gap between the train and the platform.  请注意列车与站台之间的空隙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>shun</b></td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avoid</td><td>/əˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，回避</td><td>avoid the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully avoided answering the reporter's most difficult question.  他小心地避开了记者最难的那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duck</td><td>/dʌk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避，回避；迅速低头</td><td>duck the question<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister ducked every question about the budget.  部长回避了所有关于预算的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elude</td><td>/ɪˈluːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲闪，躲避；使无法理解，使困惑</td><td>elude the police<br>躲开警察</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief eluded the police for nearly six months before being caught.  小偷躲开警方近六个月才被抓住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eschew</td><td>/ɪsˈtʃuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意避开；戒绝</td><td>eschew violence<br>戒绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks eschew luxury and live on whatever the village offers.  僧侣们避开奢华，靠村民所给的一切生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evade</td><td>/ɪˈveɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>evade the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness evaded every question about the missing money.  证人回避了所有关于失踪钱款的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 finesse</td><td>/fɪˈnes/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>娴熟技巧；（巧妙地）躲避</td><td>handle it with finesse<br>娴熟巧妙地处理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the awkward question with real finesse and everyone relaxed.  她娴熟巧妙地处理了那个尴尬的问题，大家都放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scape</td><td>/ˈskeɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>逃脱，避开</td><td>scape the worst of it<br>躲过最糟的部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village scaped the worst of the flood by sheer luck.  这个村子纯凭运气躲过了洪水最猛的部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accept</td><td>/əkˈsept/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>接受，同意，承认</td><td>accept responsibility<br>承担责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She accepted the award on behalf of her entire research team.  她代表整个研究团队接受了这个奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embrace</td><td>/ɪmˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>乐于接受；拥抱</td><td>embrace change<br>乐于接受变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young workers quickly embraced the new technology.  年轻员工迅速接受了这项新技术。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 welcome</td><td>/ˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>欢迎，乐于接受</td><td>welcome the chance to help<br>乐于接受帮忙的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Residents welcomed the decision to reopen the public library.  居民们欢迎重新开放公共图书馆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sidestep</b></td><td>/ˈsaɪdstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>回避，避开（问题）</td><td>sidestep the issue<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate neatly sidestepped the issue of rising unemployment.  这位候选人巧妙地回避了失业率上升的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beat</td><td>/ˈbiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>打，击；击败</td><td>beat him mercilessly<br>无情地打他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards beat the prisoner until the officer finally intervened.  卫兵殴打囚犯，直到那位军官出面干预。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bypass</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌpæs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>绕过，避开</td><td>bypass the rules<br>绕开规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new road bypasses the crowded center of the town.  新路绕开了拥挤的镇中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shortcut</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtˌkʌt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>捷径，快捷方式</td><td>take a shortcut<br>走捷径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a shortcut through the park to save time.  我们穿过公园走捷径以节省时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skirt</td><td>/skɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绕行，避开</td><td>skirt the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister tried to skirt the issue with a long, vague answer.  部长试图用冗长含糊的回答回避这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comply</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>遵从，服从</td><td>comply with the rules<br>遵守规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All drivers must comply with the new speed limits.  所有司机都必须遵守新的限速规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follow</td><td>/ˈfɑːloʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵循，跟随</td><td>follow the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 If you follow the instructions exactly, nothing can go wrong.  只要严格按说明操作，就不会出错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keep</td><td>/ˈkiːp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵守，坚持；保留</td><td>keep a promise<br>守约，履行承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He always keeps his promises, however inconvenient they are.  无论多不方便，他总是守约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obey</td><td>/oʊˈbeɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>服从，遵守</td><td>obey the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers must obey the speed limit in school zones.  司机在学校区域必须遵守限速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 observe</td><td>/əbˈzɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守；观察</td><td>observe the law<br>遵守法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All visitors are expected to observe the museum's rules.  所有参观者都应遵守博物馆的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>signal</b></td><td>/ˈsɪɡnəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>（在重要性、成就方面）非同寻常的；给出信号</td><td>a signal achievement<br>非同寻常的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Landing the probe safely was a signal achievement for the young team.  探测器安全着陆是这支年轻团队非同寻常的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguished</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卓越的，杰出的；高贵的</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize went to a distinguished historian of medieval law.  奖项授予一位杰出的中世纪法律史学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illustrious</td><td>/ɪˈlʌstriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>著名的，杰出的</td><td>an illustrious career<br>辉煌杰出的生涯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She retired after an illustrious career in medical research.  在医学研究领域度过杰出的生涯后，她退休了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luminous</td><td>/ˈluːmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发光的，明亮的</td><td>a luminous dial<br>夜光表盘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The luminous dial of his watch glowed faintly in the dark.  他手表的夜光表盘在黑暗中微微发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 notable</td><td>/ˈnoʊtəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显著的；值得注意的</td><td>a notable achievement<br>显著的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has been a notable drop in traffic accidents this year.  今年交通事故有显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noteworthy</td><td>/ˈnoʊˌtwɜːrði/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得注意的，显著的</td><td>a noteworthy achievement<br>一项值得注意的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most noteworthy feature of the report is its honesty.  这份报告最值得注意的地方是它的诚实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outstanding</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，突出的</td><td>an outstanding performance<br>出色的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an outstanding performance in her very first concert.  她在首场音乐会上表现出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preeminent</td><td>/priˈemɪnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>优秀的，重要的</td><td>the preeminent scholar<br>最杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is the preeminent authority on medieval trade routes in Europe.  他是研究欧洲中世纪贸易路线最杰出的权威。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prestigious</td><td>/preˈstɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有声望的，受尊敬的</td><td>a prestigious award<br>有声望的奖项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned down a prestigious post to stay near his family.  他为了留在家人身边拒绝了一个有声望的职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redoubtable</td><td>/rɪˈdaʊtəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，值得尊敬的；可怕的</td><td>a redoubtable rival<br>令人敬畏的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a redoubtable rival in the final round of the tournament.  她在锦标赛决赛轮中遇上一位令人敬畏的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beckon</td><td>/ˈbekən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>招手示意，吸引</td><td>beckon someone over<br>招手叫某人过来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The waiter beckoned us toward a table by the window.  服务员招手示意我们去靠窗的桌子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flag</td><td>/flæɡ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>变得衰弱</td><td>his energy began to flag<br>他的精力开始衰减</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His energy began to flag after the twentieth mile.  跑到第二十英里后他的精力开始衰减。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gesture</td><td>/ˈdʒestʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>手势；做手势</td><td>make a friendly gesture<br>做出友好的表示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gestured toward the door without saying a word.  她一言不发地朝门做了个手势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wave</td><td>/ˈweɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>挥手示意；波浪</td><td>wave goodbye<br>挥手告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waved to the driver to stop at the corner.  他向司机挥手示意在拐角停下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 average</td><td>/ˈævərɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平庸的，一般的；平均数</td><td>an average student<br>成绩平平的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is average but it always arrives on time.  他的活儿一般，但总能按时交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mediocre</td><td>/ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸的；质量中等偏下的</td><td>a mediocre performance<br>平庸的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's mediocre performance disappointed listeners who had waited months for the concert.  乐团平庸的表演让等了几个月的听众很失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>simpleton</b></td><td>/ˈsɪmpltən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（缺乏常识的）笨蛋</td><td>treat him as a simpleton<br>把他当傻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They treated the new clerk as a simpleton until he corrected their figures.  他们把新来的职员当傻子，直到他改正了他们的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 airhead</td><td>/ˈerˌhed/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋，没脑子的人</td><td>act like an airhead<br>表现得像个笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not treat her like an airhead; she reads constantly.  别把她当笨蛋，她一直在读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dolt</td><td>/doʊlt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>call him a dolt<br>骂他是笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not call him a dolt simply because he asked an obvious question.  别只因为他问了个显而易见的问题就骂他笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 donkey</td><td>/ˈdɑːŋki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人；驴</td><td>a stubborn donkey<br>固执的蠢驴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a donkey would sign a contract without reading it.  只有蠢人才会不读就签合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dullard</td><td>/ˈdʌlərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dullard<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher wrongly treated the quiet boy as a hopeless dullard.  老师错把这个安静的男孩当成无可救药的笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idiot</td><td>/ˈɪdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>白痴，蠢人</td><td>act like an idiot<br>表现得像蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an idiot would drive that fast in fog.  只有蠢人才会在雾中开那么快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moron</td><td>/ˈmɔːˌrɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人，笨蛋</td><td>some moron on the road<br>路上某个蠢货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some moron parked across two spaces again.  又有某个蠢货横占了两个车位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intellectual</td><td>/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>知识分子；智力的</td><td>a public intellectual<br>公共知识分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a leading intellectual in the reform movement.  她成为改革运动中的主要知识分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sage</td><td>/seɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>an ancient sage<br>古代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers walked for days to ask the old sage for advice.  村民们走了好几天去向那位老智者求教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wit</td><td>/wɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智，智慧</td><td>a flash of wit<br>一闪的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quick wit saved the interview after the microphone failed.  麦克风出故障后，是她的机敏救了那场访谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>simulate</b></td><td>/ˈsɪmjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模拟，假装</td><td>simulate battle conditions<br>模拟作战环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The training program can simulate battle conditions inside a quiet classroom.  该训练项目能在安静的教室里模拟作战环境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 act</td><td>/ˈækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>装样子，表演；行动</td><td>act for the crowd<br>为观众做样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was only acting hurt to win the crowd's sympathy.  他只是装作受伤，以博取观众同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affect</td><td>/əˈfekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>假装，装作；影响</td><td>affect indifference<br>装作漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She affected surprise, though she had known for weeks.  她装作惊讶，其实早已知情数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assume</td><td>/əˈsuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>就任，承担（职权）；假定</td><td>assume office<br>就职上任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She will assume office as director next Monday.  她将于下周一就任主任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterfeit</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərfɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>仿制的，假冒的；仿制</td><td>counterfeit banknotes<br>伪造的钞票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police seized thousands of counterfeit banknotes hidden in the delivery van.  警方缴获了藏在送货车里的数千张假钞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissemble</td><td>/dɪˈsembl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>用假象隐藏真相，掩饰</td><td>dissemble his true motive<br>掩饰他的真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to dissemble his true motive, but his trembling hands betrayed him.  他试图掩饰真实动机，但颤抖的双手泄露了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imitate</td><td>/ˈɪməˌteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模仿，仿效</td><td>imitate his accent<br>模仿他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children naturally imitate the speech of adults around them.  孩子会自然地模仿身边大人的说话方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretend</td><td>/prɪˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>假装，装作</td><td>pretend to be asleep<br>装睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pretended not to hear their mother calling.  孩子们假装没听见母亲呼唤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profess</td><td>/prəˈfes/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称，假称</td><td>profess ignorance<br>声称不知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He professed great sympathy but offered no actual help.  他声称非常同情，却没提供实际帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>selfperpetuating</b></del></td><td>/ˌselfpərˈpetʃueɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自续的，能使自身永久存在的</td><td>a selfperpetuating cycle<br>自我延续的循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poverty and poor schooling form a selfperpetuating cycle that few families ever escape.  贫困与教育落后构成一种自我延续的循环，很少有家庭能够摆脱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sensuality</b></td><td>/ˌsenʃuˈæləti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>感官享乐</td><td>the sensuality of the music<br>音乐的感官魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the warm sensuality of the late orchestral works.  评论家称赞那些晚期管弦作品温暖的感官魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carnality</td><td>/kɑrˈnæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>肉欲，淫乐</td><td>sink into carnality<br>沉溺于肉欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel condemns the empty carnality of the very rich.  小说谴责了极富者空洞的肉欲生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauchery</td><td>/dəˈbɔːtʃəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡，纵欲</td><td>a life of debauchery<br>放荡的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted his inheritance on years of reckless debauchery.  他把遗产浪费在多年放荡纵欲上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hedonism</td><td>/ˈhiːdənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>享乐主义</td><td>a life of hedonism<br>享乐主义的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics accused the novel of celebrating hedonism above every other value.  批评者指责这部小说把享乐主义置于其他一切价值之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voluptuousness</td><td>/vəˈlʌptʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>感官享乐，妖艳</td><td>the voluptuousness of the music<br>音乐的感官魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the warm voluptuousness of her singing voice.  评论家赞赏她歌声中温暖的感官魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abstinence</td><td>/ˈæbstənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>节制，禁欲</td><td>abstinence from alcohol<br>戒酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor recommended total abstinence from sugar for a month.  医生建议一个月完全戒糖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 asceticism</td><td>/əˈsɛtəˌsɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁欲主义，苦行</td><td>a life of asceticism<br>苦行的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk chose asceticism over the comforts of his family home.  这位僧人舍弃家中舒适，选择了苦行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sobriety</td><td>/səˈbraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>清醒节制；严肃</td><td>a mood of sobriety<br>严肃的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has kept his sobriety for more than ten years.  他已保持戒酒清醒十多年了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 temperance</td><td>/ˈtempərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>节制，自我克制</td><td>temperance in all things<br>凡事节制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old doctor preached temperance in eating and drinking.  这位老医生倡导饮食节制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sinecure</b></td><td>/ˈsaɪnɪkjʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拿钱不干事的职务；闲差事</td><td>a comfortable sinecure<br>舒服的闲差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The post turned out to be a comfortable sinecure with almost no duties.  那个职位原来是个几乎无事可做的舒服闲差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drudge</td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>做苦工的人；做苦工</td><td>work like a drudge<br>像苦工一样干活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drudged in the kitchen from dawn to midnight.  她从清晨到午夜在厨房里做苦工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
