<tr><td>↳反 drudgery</td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>苦工；单调、卑贱或无趣的工作</td><td>the drudgery of housework<br>家务的苦累</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Machines have removed much of the drudgery of housework in modern homes.  机器已消除了现代家庭中大部分的家务苦累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 moil</del></td><td>/mɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>骚动，混乱；苦干</td><td>the moil of the city<br>城市的喧扰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He escaped the moil of the city for a quiet island.  他逃离城市的喧扰，去了个安静的小岛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sweat</td><td>/ˈswet/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>苦活；出汗</td><td>no sweat at all<br>毫不费力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Moving those crates was a real sweat.  搬那些箱子真是件苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 toil</td><td>/ˈtɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>辛苦劳作，苦干</td><td>toil in the fields<br>在田里劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers toiled from sunrise until dark during harvest.  收获时农民从日出劳作到天黑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 travail</td><td>/ˈtræveɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>辛苦劳动</td><td>years of travail<br>多年辛劳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After months of travail, the small team finally finished the bridge.  经过数月的辛苦劳作，这个小团队终于建成了这座桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sinew</b></td><td>/ˈsɪnjuː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>力量；支柱；筋；肌腱</td><td>the sinew of the nation<br>国家的力量支柱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers were once called the sinew of the whole nation.  农民曾被称为整个国家的力量支柱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 energy</td><td>/ˈenərdʒi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精力，活力；能量</td><td>full of restless energy<br>充满旺盛的精力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children still had plenty of energy after the long hike.  长途徒步之后孩子们仍精力充沛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 firepower</td><td>/ˈfaɪrˌpaʊər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>火力；实力</td><td>considerable firepower<br>相当的火力实力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team added real firepower to its front line.  这支球队为前锋线增添了实力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 force</td><td>/ˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强迫，迫使；力量</td><td>force someone to confess<br>迫使某人认罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy rain forced them to cancel the outdoor ceremony.  大雨迫使他们取消了户外典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horsepower</td><td>/ˈhɔːrsˌpaʊər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>马力；力量</td><td>plenty of horsepower<br>充足的马力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truck lacks the horsepower for such steep hills.  这卡车缺乏爬如此陡坡的马力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 might</td><td>/ˈmaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，威力</td><td>with all his might<br>竭尽全力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pushed the stalled car with all his might.  他竭尽全力推那辆熄火的车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muscle</td><td>/ˈmʌsəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>力量，实力；肌肉</td><td>financial muscle<br>财力实力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used its muscle to force lower prices.  这家公司利用实力压低价格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 potency</td><td>/ˈpoʊtənsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，效力</td><td>the potency of the drug<br>药物的效力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vaccine loses potency if it is not kept cold.  疫苗若不冷藏就会失去效力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 power</td><td>/ˈpaʊər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>权力，力量</td><td>come to power<br>掌权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party came to power after a narrow election victory.  该党以微弱优势胜选后上台执政。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puissance</td><td>/ˈpwɪsəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>权力</td><td>the puissance of kings<br>君王的权势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chronicles celebrate the puissance of kings and their armies.  古老的编年史颂扬君王及其军队的权势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strength</td><td>/ˈstreŋkθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，强度</td><td>regain one's strength<br>恢复体力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him months to regain his strength.  他花了几个月才恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vigor</td><td>/ˈvɪɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，精力</td><td>youthful vigor<br>青春活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She argued her case with surprising vigor after a sleepless night.  一夜未眠后，她仍以惊人的活力申辩自己的主张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impotence</td><td>/ˈɪmpətəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无力，无能</td><td>a sense of impotence<br>无能为力之感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters expressed impotence in the face of rising prices.  面对物价上涨，选民表达了无能为力之感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impotency</td><td>/ˈɪmpətənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无力，无能</td><td>political impotency<br>政治上的无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crisis exposed the impotency of the old committee.  这场危机暴露了老委员会的无能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 powerlessness</td><td>/ˈpaʊərləsnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无力，无权</td><td>a sense of powerlessness<br>无力感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment bred a deep feeling of powerlessness.  长期失业催生了深深的无力感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weakness</td><td>/ˈwiːknəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>弱点，缺点</td><td>a weakness for sweets<br>对甜食的偏好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her main weakness is a reluctance to delegate work.  她的主要弱点是不愿把工作交给别人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>singe</b></td><td>/sɪndʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻微烧焦</td><td>singe the hair<br>把头发烧焦一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stood too close to the candle and singed her long hair.  她站得离蜡烛太近，把长发烧焦了一点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 char</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>烧焦，炭化</td><td>char the edges<br>把边缘烧焦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fire charred the beams but spared the roof.  大火烧焦了梁木，却保住了屋顶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scorch</td><td>/skɔːrtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>炙烤，烘干</td><td>scorch the grass brown<br>把草烤成褐色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blazing sun scorched the grass brown within a single week.  烈日一周之内就把草烤成褐色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sear</td><td>/ˈsɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>烤焦，烧灼；使枯萎</td><td>sear the meat quickly<br>快速把肉煎焦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sear the meat quickly on both sides before roasting it.  烤之前先把肉两面快速煎焦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incinerate</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪnəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>将…烧成灰烬</td><td>incinerate the waste<br>焚烧废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city incinerates medical waste in a sealed furnace.  该市在密闭炉中焚烧医疗废物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sinuous</b></td><td>/ˈsɪnjuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>蜿蜒的，迂回的</td><td>a sinuous river<br>蜿蜒的河流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sinuous river winds through the wide valley below the village.  一条蜿蜒的河流穿过村子下方宽阔的谷地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bending</td><td>/ˈbendɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>弯曲的，转弯的</td><td>a bending stretch of road<br>弯曲的一段路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bending river hid the village from view.  弯曲的河流把村子挡在视野之外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curling</td><td>/ˈkɜːrlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卷曲的，弯绕的</td><td>curling smoke<br>袅袅卷曲的烟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Curling vines covered the whole stone wall.  卷曲的藤蔓覆盖了整面石墙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curved</td><td>/ˈkɜːrvd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的，弧形的</td><td>a gently curved line<br>轻微弯曲的线条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge has a curved deck of pale stone.  这座桥有一段淡色石头的弧形桥面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curving</td><td>/ˈkɜːrvɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的，成弧形的</td><td>a curving driveway<br>弯曲的车道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A curving row of trees marked the property line.  一排弯曲的树标出了地界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 serpentine</td><td>/ˈsɜːrpəntiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的</td><td>a serpentine mountain road<br>蜿蜒的山路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We drove slowly along a serpentine mountain road above the valley.  我们沿着山谷上方蜿蜒的山路缓慢前行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tortuous</td><td>/ˈtɔːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>转弯抹角的</td><td>a tortuous argument<br>转弯抹角的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tortuous explanation left the whole committee more confused than before.  他转弯抹角的解释让整个委员会比之前更困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twisted</td><td>/ˈtwɪstɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扭曲的；反常的</td><td>a twisted plot<br>曲折离奇的情节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's twisted plot keeps readers guessing until the last page.  这部小说曲折的情节让读者猜到最后一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winding</td><td>/ˈwaɪndɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>蜿蜒的，曲折的</td><td>a winding staircase<br>蜿蜒的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A winding path led up to the old chapel.  一条蜿蜒的小路通向老教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 windy</td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straight</td><td>/ˈstreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直的，笔直的</td><td>a straight road<br>笔直的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drive straight ahead until you reach the second traffic light.  一直往前开，直到第二个红绿灯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straightaway</td><td>/ˈstreɪtəˌweɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>笔直的；直道</td><td>a long straightaway stretch<br>长长的笔直路段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cars reach top speed on the final straightaway.  赛车在最后的直道上达到最高速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>snag</b></td><td>/ˈsnæɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>障碍</td><td>hit a snag<br>遇到障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The negotiations hit an unexpected snag over the question of wages.  谈判在工资问题上遇到意外障碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hitch</td><td>/ˈhɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>小障碍，故障</td><td>without a hitch<br>毫无阻碍地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony went off without a hitch.  典礼进行得毫无阻碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 catch-pp</td><td>/kætʃ ˈtwɛnti ˈtu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>两难困境（catch-22 讹写）</td><td>a real catch-pp<br>十足的两难困境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is a catch-pp: you need experience to gain experience.  这条规定是个两难困境：要有经验才能获得经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gotcha</td><td>/ˈɡɑːtʃə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陷阱，坑人之处</td><td>a hidden gotcha<br>隐藏的坑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract has one gotcha buried in the fine print.  合同的小字里埋着一个坑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pitfall</td><td>/ˈpɪtfɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陷阱</td><td>avoid common pitfalls<br>避开常见陷阱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good guide will help beginners avoid the most common pitfalls.  好的指南能帮助新手避开最常见的陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sacrosanct</b></td><td>/ˈsækroʊsæŋkt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极为神圣的，不可侵犯的</td><td>regarded as sacrosanct<br>被视为不可侵犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In this office the lunch hour is treated as absolutely sacrosanct.  在这间办公室里，午餐时间被视为绝对不可侵犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hallowed</td><td>/ˈhæloʊd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神圣的，受尊崇的</td><td>hallowed ground<br>圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The battlefield is hallowed ground for the whole nation.  这片战场对全国而言是圣地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 holy</td><td>/ˈhoʊli/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神圣的，圣洁的</td><td>a holy place<br>圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pilgrims walk barefoot into the holy chamber.  朝圣者赤脚走进圣室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inviolable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈvaɪələbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可侵犯的，神圣的</td><td>an inviolable right<br>不可侵犯的权利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Freedom of conscience is treated here as an inviolable principle.  在这里，良心自由被视为不可侵犯的原则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sacred</td><td>/ˈseɪkrəd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神圣的，宗教的</td><td>a sacred duty<br>神圣的职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Protecting the archive was treated as a sacred duty.  保护档案被视为神圣职责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unassailable</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈseɪləbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可争辩的，无法否认的，不可亵渎的</td><td>an unassailable position<br>无法攻破的地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Decades of careful research gave her an unassailable reputation among historians.  数十年的严谨研究使她在史学界享有无可争辩的声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untouchable</td><td>/ˌʌnˈtʌtʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可接触的；不可动摇的</td><td>considered untouchable<br>被视为不可动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Senior managers at the firm seemed completely untouchable.  这家公司的高层管理者似乎完全碰不得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blasphemous</td><td>/ˈblæsfəməs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>亵渎神明的</td><td>a blasphemous joke<br>亵渎神明的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book was banned as blasphemous by church authorities.  这本书被教会当局以亵渎神明为由查禁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irreverent</td><td>/ɪˈrevərənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恭敬的，无礼的</td><td>an irreverent tone<br>不恭敬的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His irreverent humor delighted students and annoyed professors.  他不恭敬的幽默让学生开心却让教授不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 profane</td><td>/prəˈfeɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>亵渎</td><td>profane a sacred place<br>亵渎圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vandals profaned the temple by scrawling slogans on its walls.  破坏者在庙墙上乱涂标语，亵渎了圣殿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sacrilegious</td><td>/ˌsækrəˈlɪdʒəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>亵渎圣物的</td><td>a sacrilegious act<br>亵渎神圣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Smoking inside the temple was considered sacrilegious by the villagers.  在寺内吸烟被村民视为亵渎神圣的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scourge</b></td><td>/skɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>祸患，灾难；鞭子</td><td>the scourge of famine<br>饥荒之祸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Famine was the scourge of the region for a whole decade.  饥荒在整整十年间是该地区的祸患。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affliction</td><td>/əˈflɪkʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>痛苦，悲伤，折磨</td><td>suffer a lifelong affliction<br>承受终生的痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Chronic back pain was an affliction she learned to live with quietly.  慢性背痛是她学会默默承受的一种折磨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bane</td><td>/beɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>祸根，苦恼的根源</td><td>the bane of my life<br>我生活中的祸根</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Paperwork has always been the bane of his working life.  文书工作一直是他工作生活中的祸根。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curse</td><td>/ˈkɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>诅咒，咒骂</td><td>curse one's enemies<br>诅咒自己的敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old woman cursed her enemies in a trembling voice.  老妇人用颤抖的声音诅咒她的敌人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nemesis</td><td>/ˈneməsɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>克星，天罚</td><td>meet one's nemesis<br>遇到自己的克星</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rival team became his nemesis for a decade.  这支对手球队十年间成了他的克星。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benefit</td><td>/ˈbenəfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>好处，益处</td><td>bring lasting benefit<br>带来持久的好处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular exercise brings benefit to both body and mind.  规律锻炼对身心都有好处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blessing</td><td>/ˈblesɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幸事，福气</td><td>a mixed blessing<br>好坏兼有的事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The early rain was a blessing for the dry fields.  早来的雨对干旱的田地是一件幸事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boon</td><td>/buːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩惠，福利</td><td>a boon to farmers<br>对农民的福音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This solar charger is a real boon to travelling photographers.  这款太阳能充电器对旅行摄影师来说是一大福音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 felicity</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>得体，恰当；幸福</td><td>felicity of expression<br>措辞得体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays are admired for their felicity of expression.  她的散文以措辞得体而受赞赏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 godsend</td><td>/ˈɡɑːdˌsend/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天赐之物，及时的帮助</td><td>prove a godsend<br>成为天赐的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra grant was a godsend to the small clinic.  这笔额外拨款对小诊所是天赐之助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manna</td><td>/ˈmænə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外的恩赐</td><td>manna from heaven<br>天降的恩赐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The donation felt like manna to the struggling school.  这笔捐款对困难的学校像是天降恩赐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 windfall</td><td>/ˈwɪndfɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外之财；意外的好运</td><td>an unexpected windfall<br>意外之财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected windfall allowed them to repay the whole loan early.  这笔意外之财让他们提前还清了全部贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>skimp</b></td><td>/skɪmp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>节省花费，吝惜；克扣</td><td>skimp on materials<br>在材料上省钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Builders who skimp on materials always create problems for later owners.  在材料上省钱的建筑商总会给后来的业主留下麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 exiguous</del></td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪɡjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极少的，微薄的</td><td>an exiguous budget<br>少得可怜的预算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They survived a whole year on exiguous rations of rice.  他们靠少得可怜的米粮过了整整一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meager</td><td>/ˈmiːɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫乏的</td><td>a meager income<br>微薄的收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They raised four children on a meager income from a small roadside shop.  他们靠路边小店微薄的收入养育了四个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggardly</td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，少得可怜的</td><td>a niggardly raise<br>少得可怜的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered a niggardly raise after record profits.  在创纪录盈利后公司给了少得可怜的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poor</td><td>/pʊr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的，贫乏的；贫穷的</td><td>poor rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harvest was poor after a whole summer without rain.  整个夏天没下雨，收成很差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scant</td><td>/skænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，缺乏的</td><td>scant attention<br>很少的关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report paid scant attention to the concerns of local residents.  报告对当地居民的关切几乎没有给予关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scanty</td><td>/ˈskænti/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏的，稀少的</td><td>scanty evidence<br>证据不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Evidence for the theory remains scanty.  支持该理论的证据仍然很少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scarce</td><td>/ˈskers/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀缺的，不足的</td><td>in scarce supply<br>供应短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh water became scarce during the drought.  旱灾期间淡水变得稀缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skimpy</td><td>/ˈskɪmpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，少得可怜的</td><td>a skimpy portion of meat<br>少得可怜的一份肉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report gave only skimpy evidence for its bold claims.  报告只给出很少的证据来支持大胆的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slender</td><td>/ˈslendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>苗条的，细长的</td><td>a slender waist<br>纤细的腰身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She held the flower by its slender stem.  她捏着那朵花细长的茎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slim</td><td>/ˈslɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，微薄的；苗条的</td><td>a slim chance of success<br>极小的成功机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We have only a slim chance of catching the last train.  我们赶上末班车的机会很小。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparing</td><td>/ˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的，节约的</td><td>sparing use of salt<br>节省地用盐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was sparing with salt because of his doctor's strict orders.  遵医嘱，他用盐十分节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparse</td><td>/spɑːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀疏的，稀少的</td><td>sparse vegetation<br>稀疏的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vegetation grows sparse as the trail climbs above the tree line.  小径爬上林线以上后，植被变得稀疏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stingy</td><td>/ˈstɪndʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>小气的，吝啬的</td><td>stingy with money<br>在钱上很吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is stingy with money but surprisingly generous with his time.  他在钱上吝啬，在时间上却出奇大方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pinch</td><td>/ˈpɪntʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>短缺，窘迫；捏</td><td>feel the pinch<br>感到经济窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small shops began to feel the pinch as rents rose sharply.  租金猛涨后小店铺开始感到经济窘迫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 save</td><td>/ˈseɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拯救，挽救；节省</td><td>save him from danger<br>把他从危险中救出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lifeguard saved two swimmers from the strong current.  救生员从急流中救出两名游泳者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrimp</td><td>/skrɪmp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>节省；吝啬；紧缩开支</td><td>scrimp and save<br>省吃俭用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had to scrimp and save for years to buy the shop.  他们省吃俭用多年才买下这家店铺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spare</td><td>/ˈsper/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>宽恕，免除；备用的</td><td>spare his life<br>饶他一命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The victors spared everyone who surrendered before nightfall.  胜利者宽恕了所有在天黑前投降的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stint</td><td>/stɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>吝惜，节省</td><td>stint on materials<br>在材料上省钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The builder refused to stint on materials even when costs rose.  即便成本上升，这位建筑商也不肯在材料上省钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abundant</td><td>/əˈbʌndənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>abundant evidence<br>大量的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water was abundant after the spring rains.  春雨过后水源充足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ample</td><td>/ˈæmpəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充足的，富裕的</td><td>ample opportunity<br>充足的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is ample room for everyone in the van.  车里有足够的空间容纳所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bountiful</td><td>/ˈbaʊntɪfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充裕的</td><td>a bountiful harvest<br>丰盛的收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The valley gave them a bountiful crop of apples.  这个山谷给了他们丰盛的苹果收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 copious</td><td>/ˈkoʊpiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>take copious notes<br>做大量笔记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took copious notes during every lecture and reread them each night.  她每堂课都记大量笔记，并在每晚重读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 generous</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的；慷慨的</td><td>a generous helping<br>满满的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She poured a generous glass of wine for each guest.  她给每位客人倒了满满一杯酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 plenteous</del></td><td>/ˈplɛntiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充足的</td><td>a plenteous supply of water<br>充足的水源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That year brought plenteous rain and unusually heavy crops.  那年雨水充足，庄稼格外茂盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plentiful</td><td>/ˈplentəfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的</td><td>a plentiful harvest<br>丰收</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain came early, so the harvest was unusually plentiful.  雨来得早，所以收成异常丰富。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dissipate</td><td>/ˈdɪsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>驱散，消散；浪费</td><td>dissipate the fog<br>驱散雾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning mist dissipated as soon as the sun rose.  太阳一升起，晨雾就消散了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 prodigalize</del></td><td>/ˈprɑdəɡəˌlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挥霍，浪费</td><td>prodigalize the family fortune<br>挥霍家产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He prodigalized his inheritance on cars and long holidays.  他把遗产挥霍在汽车和长假上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 squander</td><td>/ˈskwɑːndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浪费</td><td>squander an opportunity<br>浪费机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not squander this opportunity to study abroad for a year.  不要浪费这次出国留学一年的机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waste</td><td>/ˈweɪst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>荒芜的；浪费；废物</td><td>waste land<br>荒地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on this waste ground beside the old factory.  旧工厂旁的这片荒地上什么也长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scruple</b></td><td>/ˈskruːpl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（良心上的）不安</td><td>have no scruples<br>毫无顾忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt no scruple about reading the private letters of his brother.  他对偷看弟弟的私人信件毫无良心不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compunction</td><td>/kəmˈpʌŋkʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>内疚，良心不安</td><td>without compunction<br>毫无内疚地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She fired the loyal staff without the slightest compunction.  她毫无内疚地解雇了那些忠诚的员工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misgiving</td><td>/ˌmɪsˈɡɪvɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>担忧，疑虑</td><td>have serious misgivings<br>深感疑虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had serious misgivings about signing the contract without a lawyer present.  她对在没有律师在场的情况下签合同深感疑虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assurance</td><td>/əˈʃʊrəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>保证；自信，确信</td><td>give an assurance<br>作出保证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered the difficult questions with surprising assurance.  她以惊人的自信回答了那些难题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 certainty</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtənti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确定，必然；确信</td><td>with absolute certainty<br>绝对确定地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one can predict the outcome with absolute certainty.  没人能绝对确定地预测结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 certitude</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信无疑</td><td>moral certitude<br>道德上的确信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke of the plan's success with unshakable certitude.  他以不可动摇的确信谈起这项计划的成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confidence</td><td>/ˈkɑːnfədəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信心，确信</td><td>full of confidence<br>充满信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question with quiet confidence.  他从容而自信地回答了每个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conviction</td><td>/kənˈvɪkʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>深信，确信；证明有罪</td><td>speak with conviction<br>满怀信念地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She argued her case with a conviction that silenced every critic.  她以坚定的信念陈述自己的主张，令所有批评者沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sureness</td><td>/ˈʃʊrnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信；稳准</td><td>the sureness of his touch<br>他手法的稳准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played the difficult passage with remarkable sureness.  她极其稳准地演奏了那个难段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>skirt</b></td><td>/skɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绕行，避开</td><td>skirt the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister tried to skirt the issue with a long, vague answer.  部长试图用冗长含糊的回答回避这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bypass</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌpæs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>绕过，避开</td><td>bypass the rules<br>绕开规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new road bypasses the crowded center of the town.  新路绕开了拥挤的镇中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circumvent</td><td>/ˌsɜːrkəmˈvent/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲避，不遵从</td><td>circumvent the rules<br>规避规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some firms hire consultants to circumvent the new tax rules.  一些公司聘请顾问来规避新的税收规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detour</td><td>/ˈdiːtʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>绕道，迂路；偏离</td><td>take a long detour<br>绕一段远路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Roadworks forced us to take a long detour through the hills.  道路施工迫使我们绕道穿越丘陵走了很远。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shortcut</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtˌkʌt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>捷径，快捷方式</td><td>take a shortcut<br>走捷径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a shortcut through the park to save time.  我们穿过公园走捷径以节省时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sidestep</td><td>/ˈsaɪdstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>回避，避开（问题）</td><td>sidestep the issue<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate neatly sidestepped the issue of rising unemployment.  这位候选人巧妙地回避了失业率上升的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pursue</td><td>/pərˈsuː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>追求，追寻；追捕</td><td>pursue a career<br>追求事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She pursued him for months before he finally agreed to dinner.  她追了他好几个月，他才终于答应共进晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seek</td><td>/ˈsiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>寻求，追求</td><td>seek professional advice<br>寻求专业建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many families seek shelter in the school gym during heavy storms.  暴风雨期间许多家庭到学校体育馆寻求庇护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slack</b></td><td>/slæk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松弛的；疏忽的，大意的</td><td>slack about deadlines<br>对截止日期松懈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new manager was slack about deadlines and lost several clients.  新经理对截止日期很松懈，因此丢了好几个客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insecure</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不牢固的，不稳固的；缺乏自信的</td><td>an insecure ladder<br>一把不牢固的梯子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shelf was so insecure that it collapsed under the books.  那个架子太不牢固，被书压塌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lax</td><td>/læks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松懈的，不严格的；漫不经心的</td><td>lax security standards<br>松懈的安保标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lax security standards at the warehouse allowed the theft to go unnoticed.  仓库松懈的安保标准使这起盗窃未被发觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loosened</td><td>/ˈluːsənd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>松开的，放松的</td><td>a loosened knot<br>松开的绳结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked in with a loosened tie and tired eyes.  他领带松着、眼神疲惫地走进来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relaxed</td><td>/rɪˈlækst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>松弛的，放松的</td><td>a relaxed grip<br>放松的握力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her shoulders finally looked relaxed after the massage.  按摩后她的肩膀终于显得松弛了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slackened</td><td>/ˈslækənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松弛的，放松的</td><td>a slackened rope<br>松弛的绳子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slackened sail flapped uselessly in the calm.  松垂的帆在无风中徒然拍动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsecured</td><td>/ˌʌnsɪˈkjʊrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未固定的，松脱的</td><td>an unsecured load<br>未固定的货物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unsecured ladder slid off the roof of the van.  一把未固定的梯子从货车顶上滑落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derelict</td><td>/ˈderəlɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>玩忽职守的，不认真的</td><td>derelict in his duty<br>玩忽职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard was found derelict in his duty on the night of the theft.  那名警卫被认定在失窃当夜玩忽职守。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disregardful</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑrdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不顾的，漠视的</td><td>disregardful of safety<br>漠视安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver was disregardful of every posted speed limit.  这名司机漠视每一处限速标志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lazy</td><td>/ˈleɪzi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的</td><td>too lazy to cook<br>懒得做饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too lazy to walk the dog on cold mornings.  寒冷的早晨他懒得去遛狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglectful</td><td>/nɪˈɡlektfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽的，不留心的</td><td>neglectful of his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report found the agency neglectful of basic safety inspections.  报告认定该机构疏忽了基本安全检查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglecting</td><td>/nɪˈɡlektɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>忽视的，怠慢的</td><td>neglecting his studies<br>忽视学业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the term gaming, neglecting every assignment he received.  他整学期打游戏，忽视了收到的每项作业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negligent</td><td>/ˈneɡlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽大意的</td><td>negligent in his duties<br>对职责疏忽大意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company was found negligent in maintaining its equipment.  该公司被认定在设备维护上存在疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remiss</td><td>/rɪˈmɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽的，不留心的</td><td>be remiss in one's duties<br>疏于职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I would be remiss if I did not thank the volunteers.  如果我不感谢志愿者，那就是我的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taut</td><td>/tɔːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧绷的</td><td>pull the rope taut<br>把绳子拉紧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wire was so taut that it hummed in the wind.  这根线绷得很紧，在风中嗡嗡作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tense</td><td>/ˈtens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧绷的，紧张的</td><td>tense shoulders<br>紧绷的肩膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tense jaw showed how hard he was concentrating.  他紧绷的下颌显示出他多么专注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tight</td><td>/ˈtaɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的；紧的</td><td>tight with money<br>在钱上很抠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her uncle was famously tight with money all his life.  她舅舅一生在钱上出名地抠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attentive</td><td>/əˈtentɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，专注的；周到的</td><td>an attentive audience<br>专注的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse stayed attentive to every change in his breathing.  护士留心他呼吸的每一点变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conscientious</td><td>/ˌkɑːnʃiˈenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仔细的，一丝不苟的；有良心的，正直的</td><td>a conscientious worker<br>一位认真的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A conscientious editor checks every fact before the article goes to print.  一丝不苟的编辑会在文章付印前核实每一个事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ful</td><td>/fʊl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后缀：充满……的</td><td>the suffix -ful in careful<br>careful 中的后缀 -ful</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suffix ful means full of the quality named.  后缀 ful 表示充满所指的性质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slake</b></td><td>/sleɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解渴；满足（欲望）</td><td>slake one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They stopped at the well to slake their thirst after the long climb.  长途攀登后他们在井边停下解渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quench</td><td>/kwentʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解（渴）；熄灭</td><td>quench one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A glass of cold lemonade quickly quenched my desperate thirst.  一杯冰镇柠檬水很快解了我的焦渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sate</td><td>/seɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使饱足，充分满足</td><td>sate one's hunger<br>充分满足饥饿感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single bowl of thin soup was not enough to sate his hunger.  一碗清汤不足以满足他的饥饿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satiate</td><td>/ˈseɪʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>（使）饱足（的），过分满足（的）</td><td>satiate his curiosity<br>充分满足他的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No amount of reading could satiate the curiosity of that boy about stars.  再多的阅读也无法满足那男孩对星星的好奇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfy</td><td>/ˈsætɪsfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满意；使确信</td><td>satisfy the skeptics<br>让怀疑者信服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra evidence satisfied the skeptics who had doubted his story.  额外的证据让怀疑他说法的人信服了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tantalize</td><td>/ˈtæntəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，挑逗，引诱</td><td>tantalize with promises<br>用承诺引诱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of roast meat tantalized the hungry travelers.  烤肉的香味挑逗着饥饿的旅人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tease</td><td>/ˈtiːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>取笑，戏弄；逗弄</td><td>tease his little sister<br>戏弄他的妹妹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boys teased their little sister about her new haircut.  男孩们拿妹妹的新发型取笑她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slant</b></td><td>/slænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（使）倾斜；斜度</td><td>the floor slants slightly<br>地面略微倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old wooden floor slants slightly toward the far corner.  这块旧木地板向远处的角落略微倾斜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 angle</td><td>/ˈæŋɡəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使倾斜；角度</td><td>angle the mirror<br>把镜子调斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He angled the lamp so the light fell on the page.  他把灯调斜，使光照在书页上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outlook</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌlʊk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>观点，看法；前景</td><td>a positive outlook<br>积极的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His outlook on the economy changed after the report.  报告出来后他对经济的看法变了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perspective</td><td>/pərˈspektɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>视角，观点</td><td>from a different perspective<br>从不同的视角</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film shows the war from a soldier's perspective.  这部电影从士兵的视角展现战争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 standpoint</td><td>/ˈstændˌpɔɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>立场，观点</td><td>from a legal standpoint<br>从法律的立场看</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From a safety standpoint the design is unacceptable.  从安全立场看这个设计不可接受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 viewpoint</td><td>/ˈvjuːˌpɔɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>观点，看法</td><td>express a viewpoint<br>表达一种观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each panelist offered a sharply different viewpoint.  每位嘉宾都提出截然不同的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 canted</td><td>/ˈkæntɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，斜置的</td><td>a canted roof<br>倾斜的屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The camera used a canted angle to unsettle viewers.  镜头用倾斜角度让观众感到不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 graded</td><td>/ˈɡreɪdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有坡度的，分级的</td><td>a gently graded slope<br>缓缓倾斜的坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path is graded so wheelchairs can climb it.  这条路有坡度设计，轮椅可以上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inclined</td><td>/ˌɪnˈklaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾向于的，愿意的</td><td>inclined to believe<br>倾向于相信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I am inclined to believe his version of the story.  我倾向于相信他讲的那个版本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leaning</td><td>/ˈliːnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>倾斜的；倾向</td><td>a leaning tower<br>倾斜的塔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaning wall was propped up with heavy timbers.  倾斜的墙用粗木支撑起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 listing</td><td>/ˈlɪstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>（船）倾斜的；清单</td><td>a listing ship<br>倾斜的船</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The listing vessel was towed slowly back into the harbor.  那艘倾斜的船被慢慢拖回港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oblique</td><td>/əˈbliːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>斜的</td><td>an oblique angle<br>斜角</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The roof meets the wall at an oblique angle that complicates repairs.  屋顶与墙以一个斜角相接，使修理变得复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pitched</td><td>/ˈpɪtʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，有斜度的</td><td>a steeply pitched roof<br>陡斜的屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Snow slides easily off the steeply pitched roof.  雪很容易从陡斜的屋顶滑落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raked</td><td>/ˈreɪkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，斜置的</td><td>a raked stage<br>前低后高的斜舞台</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theater has a raked floor so everyone can see.  剧院地面是倾斜的，让所有人都能看见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slanted</td><td>/ˈslæntɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的；有偏向的</td><td>slanted news coverage<br>有偏向的新闻报道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was clearly slanted in favor of the company.  这份报告明显偏向该公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sloping</td><td>/ˈsloʊpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，有坡度的</td><td>a sloping roof<br>斜屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain ran quickly off the steeply sloping roof.  雨水迅速从陡斜的屋顶流下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tilting</td><td>/ˈtɪltɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>倾斜的；倾斜</td><td>tilting at an angle<br>以一定角度倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tilting floor made the old farmhouse feel unsettling.  倾斜的地板让这座老农舍显得令人不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perpendicular</td><td>/ˌpɜːrpənˈdɪkjələr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>垂直的；垂线</td><td>perpendicular to the ground<br>与地面垂直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Draw a line perpendicular to the base of the triangle.  画一条与三角形底边垂直的直线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plumb</td><td>/plʌm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>完全的；深入探究，探测</td><td>plumb the depths of<br>深入探究…的深处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The biographer tried to plumb the depths of that private grief.  传记作者试图深入探究那份私人悲伤的深处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vertical</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>垂直的，竖直的</td><td>a sheer vertical drop<br>陡直的垂直落差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cliff presented a nearly vertical face to the climbers.  峭壁向攀登者呈现出几乎垂直的岩面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slate</b></td><td>/sleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>列入名单，计划，安排</td><td>slated for demolition<br>被安排拆除</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old factory is slated for demolition early next spring.  这座旧工厂定于明年初春拆除。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catalog</td><td>/ˈkætəlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>编入目录，登记；目录</td><td>catalog the new books<br>为新书编目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Librarians catalog every donated volume before shelving it.  图书馆员在上架前为每本捐赠图书编目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enroll</td><td>/ɪnˈroʊl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>注册，报名入学</td><td>enroll in a course<br>报名参加课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She enrolled in evening classes to finish her degree.  她报名上夜校以完成学位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enter</td><td>/ˈentər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>记入，登录；进入</td><td>enter the data<br>录入数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please enter your name in the register at the door.  请把你的名字登记在门口的簿子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 index</td><td>/ˈɪndeks/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>编入索引；索引</td><td>index every chapter<br>为每章编索引</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor will index all the proper names in the book.  编辑将为书中所有专有名词编制索引。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscribe</td><td>/ɪnˈskraɪb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>登记，注册；铭刻</td><td>inscribe his name<br>把他的名字登记上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name was inscribed in the college register that autumn.  那年秋天他的名字被登记进学院名册。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 record</td><td>/rəˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>记录，登记；记录</td><td>record the minutes<br>记录会议纪要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk recorded every decision made at the meeting.  书记员记录了会上做出的每一项决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 register</td><td>/ˈredʒɪstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>登记，记录</td><td>register a complaint<br>登记一项投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You must register your bicycle with the city office.  你必须在市政办公室登记自行车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schedule</td><td>/ˈskedʒʊl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>安排，排定；日程表</td><td>schedule a meeting<br>安排一次会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They scheduled the hearing for early next month.  他们把听证会安排在下月初。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delete</td><td>/dɪˈliːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>删除，去掉</td><td>delete the entry<br>删除该条目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deleted her name from the volunteer list.  她把自己的名字从志愿者名单上删掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 erase</td><td>/ɪˈreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>擦除，抹去</td><td>erase the pencil marks<br>擦掉铅笔痕迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to erase every trace of the message from the whiteboard.  他试图把白板上那条信息的痕迹全部擦掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slew</b></td><td>/sluː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，许多</td><td>a slew of problems<br>一大堆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new policy generated a slew of complaints from local business owners.  新政策引来当地商户的一大堆抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered an abundance of fresh fruit at low prices.  市场以低价供应大量新鲜水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bundle</td><td>/ˈbʌndəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>匆忙送走，急送</td><td>bundle him into a car<br>把他匆匆塞进车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards bundled the prisoner into a waiting van.  卫兵把犯人匆匆塞进等候的货车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dozen</td><td>/ˈdʌzən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>许多，一打</td><td>dozens of complaints<br>数十起投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dozens of complaints arrived within the first week.  第一周内就来了数十起投诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 multiplicity</td><td>/ˌmʌltəˈplɪsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>多样性，多重性</td><td>a multiplicity of causes<br>多重原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failure resulted from a multiplicity of small errors.  这次失败源于多种小错误的叠加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 myriad</td><td>/ˈmɪriəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的，大量的</td><td>myriad possibilities<br>无数的可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new technology opens myriad possibilities for teaching students in remote villages.  这项新技术为教偏远村庄的学生打开了无数可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 plentitude</del></td><td>/ˈplɛnɪtud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，大量</td><td>a plentitude of talent<br>人才济济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The region enjoys a plentitude of water and fertile soil.  该地区拥有充足的水源和肥沃的土壤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profusion</td><td>/prəˈfjuːʒn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>a profusion of flowers<br>繁花盛开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A profusion of wildflowers covered the hillside after the spring rains.  春雨过后，山坡上开满了大量野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scad</td><td>/ˈskæd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，许多</td><td>scads of money<br>大量的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He earned scads of money from that single invention.  他靠那一项发明赚了大量的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shipload</td><td>/ˈʃɪˌploʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一船的量；大量</td><td>a shipload of grain<br>一船谷物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shipload of grain reached the harbor before dawn.  黎明前一船谷物运抵港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 volume</td><td>/ˈvɑːljum/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量；容量；卷</td><td>a great volume of traffic<br>巨大的车流量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge cannot handle this volume of traffic.  这座桥承受不了如此大的车流量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wealth</td><td>/ˈwelθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，丰富；财富</td><td>a wealth of experience<br>丰富的经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor brought a wealth of experience to the team.  这位老编辑给团队带来了丰富的经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ace</td><td>/ˈeɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>高手，能人</td><td>an ace at chess<br>国际象棋高手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is an ace with numbers and finished the audit alone.  他是数字高手，独自完成了审计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bit</td><td>/ˈbɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一点，少量</td><td>a bit of luck<br>一点运气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Add just a bit of salt to the simmering sauce.  往煮着的酱汁里加一点点盐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 driblet</del></td><td>/ˈdrɪblət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，点滴</td><td>in driblets<br>一点一点地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 News of the accident arrived in driblets all evening.  事故的消息整晚一点一点地传来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glimmer</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一丝；微光</td><td>a glimmer of hope<br>一丝希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter offered a faint glimmer of hope.  那封信带来一丝微弱的希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handful</td><td>/ˈhændˌfʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少数，一把</td><td>only a handful of guests<br>只有少数客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a handful of guests stayed until the end.  只有少数客人留到最后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hint</td><td>/ˈhɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暗示，提示</td><td>drop a hint<br>透露一点暗示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave me a subtle hint about the surprise party.  她微妙地暗示了那个惊喜派对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mite</td><td>/maɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微小的东西，很少的钱</td><td>a mite of comfort<br>一点点安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brief letter offered a mite of comfort during those difficult winter months.  他简短的信在那些艰难的冬月里带来了一点点安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mouthful</td><td>/ˈmaʊθˌfʊl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一口；一大堆话</td><td>a mouthful of water<br>一口水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swallowed a mouthful of cold water and kept running.  他喝了一口凉水，继续奔跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nip</td><td>/nɪp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小口吃喝</td><td>a nip of whisky<br>一小口威士忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He poured himself a nip of whisky and sat down by the fire.  他给自己倒了一小口威士忌，坐到火边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ounce</td><td>/ˈaʊns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一点；盎司</td><td>every ounce of strength<br>每一分力气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used every ounce of strength to lift the beam.  他用尽每一分力气抬起那根梁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pittance</td><td>/ˈpɪtns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量津贴</td><td>work for a pittance<br>为微薄报酬工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The apprentices worked long hours for a pittance and no benefits.  学徒们长时间工作，只拿微薄津贴且没有福利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sprinkle</td><td>/ˈsprɪŋkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>少量，星星点点</td><td>a sprinkle of sugar<br>少量的糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cook added only a sprinkle of sugar to the batter.  厨师只往面糊里加了少量的糖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trace</td><td>/ˈtreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痕迹；微量</td><td>a trace of irony<br>一丝讽意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tea showed only a trace of bitterness.  这茶只有一丝苦味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slight</b></td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiddling</td><td>/ˈfɪdlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的，琐碎的</td><td>fiddling details<br>琐碎的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not waste an afternoon on such fiddling corrections.  别为这些琐碎的修改浪费一个下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 little</td><td>/ˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，少的</td><td>a little detail<br>一个小细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed one little detail that everyone else had missed.  她注意到一个别人都漏掉的小细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minor</td><td>/ˈmaɪnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>较小的，不重要的</td><td>a minor problem<br>一个小问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only minor repairs were needed after the small accident.  小事故后只需要一些小修理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nugatory</del></td><td>/ˈnuːɡətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的</td><td>a nugatory objection<br>无关紧要的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court dismissed his nugatory objection and proceeded with the hearing.  法庭驳回了他无关紧要的反对，继续进行听证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contemn</del></td><td>/kənˈtem/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，蔑视</td><td>contemn the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed to contemn every tradition the school held dear.  他似乎蔑视学校珍视的每一项传统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disdain</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻视，鄙视</td><td>disdain to answer<br>不屑于回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She disdained the flattery of her ambitious colleagues.  她鄙视那些野心勃勃的同事的奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disrespect</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>轻蔑，不敬；不尊重</td><td>show disrespect for elders<br>对长辈不敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Interrupting her that way showed real disrespect.  那样打断她实在很不尊重人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snub</td><td>/snʌb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，不理睬</td><td>deliberately snub a colleague<br>故意冷落同事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deliberately snubbed her colleague by ignoring him at the meeting.  她在会上故意不理睬同事，冷落了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 look down</td><td>/ˈlʊk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>轻视，看不起</td><td>look down on him<br>看不起他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She never looked down on people with less education.  她从不看不起受教育较少的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bypass</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌpæs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>绕过，避开</td><td>bypass the rules<br>绕开规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new road bypasses the crowded center of the town.  新路绕开了拥挤的镇中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overpass</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌpæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>忽略，越过</td><td>overpass minor faults<br>忽略小的过失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor overpassed several errors in the final draft.  编辑忽略了终稿中的几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrug</td><td>/ʃrʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，忽略</td><td>shrug off criticism<br>对批评不予理会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The authorities simply shrugged off criticism from local residents.  当局对当地居民的批评一笑置之。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slur</td><td>/slɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说；诋毁，污辱</td><td>slur one's words<br>说话含糊不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so tired that he began to slur his words.  他累得开始说话含糊不清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eventful</td><td>/ɪˈventfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多事的，重要的</td><td>an eventful year<br>多事的一年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It has been an eventful year for the whole family.  对全家来说这是多事的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meaningful</td><td>/ˈmiːnɪŋfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有意义的，意味深长的</td><td>meaningful work<br>有意义的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left banking to find more meaningful work.  她离开银行业去寻找更有意义的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revere</td><td>/rɪˈvɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（尤指对神的）尊崇，尊敬</td><td>revered as a hero<br>被尊崇为英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students in the village still revere the teacher who founded their school.  村里的学生仍然尊崇创办他们学校的那位老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerate</td><td>/ˈvenəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>尊敬</td><td>venerate their ancestors<br>崇敬他们的祖先</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers still venerate their ancestors with offerings twice each year.  村民每年两次以供品崇敬他们的祖先。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heed</td><td>/ˈhiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>注意，留心</td><td>heed the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few sailors heeded the warning about the coming storm.  很少有水手留意关于风暴来临的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mind</td><td>/ˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>留意，注意；介意</td><td>mind the step<br>留意台阶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please mind the gap between the train and the platform.  请注意列车与站台之间的空隙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sling</b></td><td>/slɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>投掷</td><td>sling a stone<br>投掷石块</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not sling that heavy bag onto the delicate glass table.  别把那个重包扔到脆弱的玻璃桌上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cast</td><td>/kæst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出；抛弃</td><td>cast doubt on<br>对…提出质疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new evidence cast doubt on the official explanation.  新证据让官方的解释受到质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chuck</td><td>/ˈtʃʌk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扔，抛；丢弃</td><td>chuck it in the bin<br>把它扔进垃圾桶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He chucked the empty can into the recycling bin.  他把空罐子扔进了回收箱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dash</td><td>/ˈdæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>猛冲，疾奔</td><td>dash across the street<br>快速冲过街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dashed across the street to catch the departing bus.  她冲过街道去赶那辆即将开走的公交车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fire</td><td>/ˈfaɪər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解雇；开火；火</td><td>fire an idle worker<br>解雇懒散的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager fired two clerks after the audit found losses.  审计查出亏损后经理解雇了两名职员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fling</td><td>/ˈflɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>扔，猛掷；一时放纵</td><td>fling the door open<br>猛地推开门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She flung her coat onto the chair and sat down.  她把外套一甩扔到椅子上坐下了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heave</td><td>/ˈhiːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>用力举起，用力扔</td><td>heave a heavy sack<br>用力扔沉重的袋子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers heaved the sandbags onto the waiting truck.  工人们把沙袋用力扔上等候的卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurl</td><td>/ˈhɜːrl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>猛掷，用力投</td><td>hurl a rock<br>猛掷石头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Protesters hurled stones at the armored vehicles.  抗议者向装甲车猛掷石块。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurtle</td><td>/ˈhɜːrtəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>猛冲，飞驰；猛投</td><td>hurtle down the hill<br>飞速冲下山坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sled hurtled down the icy slope at frightening speed.  雪橇以骇人的速度冲下冰坡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 launch</td><td>/ˈlɔːntʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>发起，开始；发射</td><td>launch a new campaign<br>发起新的活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to launch its cheapest model this autumn.  公司计划今秋推出其最便宜的型号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lob</td><td>/ˈlɑːb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>高抛，抛高球</td><td>lob the ball over<br>把球高高抛过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She lobbed the ball gently over the net.  她把球轻轻高抛过网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loft</td><td>/ˈlɔːft/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>把球高高击起；阁楼</td><td>loft the ball high<br>把球高高击起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The golfer lofted the ball onto the distant green.  高尔夫球手把球高高击上远处的果岭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pelt</td><td>/ˈpelt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>连续投掷；毛皮</td><td>pelt with snowballs<br>用雪球投掷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pelted each other with snowballs all afternoon.  孩子们整个下午互相投掷雪球。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pitch</td><td>/ˈpɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（船、飞机）颠簸倾斜</td><td>pitch in heavy seas<br>在大浪中颠簸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boat pitched so badly that dishes slid off.  船颠簸得厉害，盘子都滑落了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toss</td><td>/ˈtɔːs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>扔，抛；抛弃</td><td>toss aside<br>随手丢在一边</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She tossed the crumpled letter into the fire without reading it.  她没读就把那封皱信扔进了火里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 catch</td><td>/ˈkætʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>染上（疾病）；抓住</td><td>catch a cold<br>感冒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You will catch a cold if you go out with wet hair.  你要是湿着头发出门就会感冒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slippery</b></td><td>/ˈslɪpəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光滑的；意义不明确的</td><td>a slippery road<br>滑溜的路面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The marble stairs became dangerously slippery after the heavy morning rain.  一场晨雨过后，大理石台阶滑得危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 greased</td><td>/ˈɡriːst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>涂了油的，滑的</td><td>a greased pole<br>涂了油的杆子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greased hinge no longer squeaked when the door moved.  上了油的合页在门开合时不再吱吱作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 greasy</td><td>/ˈɡriːsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油腻的，滑溜的</td><td>greasy fingers<br>油腻的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wiped his greasy fingers on a paper napkin.  他把油腻的手指擦在纸巾上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lubricated</td><td>/ˈluːbrəˌkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>润滑的，加了油的</td><td>a well lubricated engine<br>润滑良好的引擎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A properly lubricated chain lasts far longer than a dry one.  润滑良好的链条比干燥的耐用得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oiled</td><td>/ˈɔɪld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>上了油的，滑润的</td><td>a well oiled machine<br>运转顺畅的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team worked like a well oiled machine all season.  整个赛季这支队伍运转得像上好油的机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slicked</td><td>/ˈslɪkt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>梳光的，弄光滑的</td><td>hair slicked back<br>头发向后梳光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He appeared onstage with his hair slicked back neatly.  他上台时头发整齐地向后梳光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slithery</td><td>/ˈslɪðəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>滑溜的，易滑的</td><td>slithery wet rocks<br>湿滑的岩石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slithery mud made every step along the bank treacherous.  滑溜的泥浆使沿岸每一步都很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elusive</td><td>/ɪˈluːsɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以捉摸的；难抓到的</td><td>an elusive meaning<br>难以捉摸的含义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The precise meaning of the poem remains elusive to critics.  这首诗的确切含义仍让评论家难以捉摸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evasive</td><td>/ɪˈveɪzɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含糊其辞的，回避的</td><td>an evasive answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave an evasive answer to every question.  部长对每个问题都给出含糊的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fugitive</td><td>/ˈfjuːdʒətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>短暂的，易逝的；逃亡的</td><td>a fugitive impression<br>转瞬即逝的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fugitive smile crossed her face before she resumed her serious expression.  一丝转瞬即逝的笑意掠过她的脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unintelligible</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnˈtelədʒəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的，费解的</td><td>unintelligible mumbling<br>含混不清的嘟囔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The recording was so noisy that the speech became unintelligible.  录音噪声太大，讲话内容变得无法听懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vague</td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rough</td><td>/ˈrʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，粗暴的；艰苦的</td><td>a rough surface<br>粗糙的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road grew rough as we climbed into the hills.  我们爬上山时路变得坎坷不平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apparent</td><td>/əˈperənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的；表面上的</td><td>for no apparent reason<br>没有明显原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It soon became apparent that the machine had never worked.  很快就明显看出这台机器从未运转过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 evident</td><td>/ˈevɪdənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显然的</td><td>clearly evident<br>显而易见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her talent for music was evident from a very early age.  她的音乐才华从很小就显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 palpable</td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unambiguous</td><td>/ˌʌnæmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，毫不含糊的</td><td>unambiguous written instructions<br>明确的书面指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract gives unambiguous rules about late payment.  合同就迟付款给出了明确规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unequivocal</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不含糊的，明确的</td><td>an unequivocal denial<br>毫不含糊的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board issued an unequivocal statement supporting the director.  董事会发表了明确支持主任的声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slipshod</b></td><td>/ˈslɪpʃɑːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，随意的</td><td>slipshod workmanship<br>马虎的做工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inspector criticized the slipshod workmanship on the newly built bridge.  检查员批评新建桥梁马虎的做工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 botchy</del></td><td>/ˈbɑːtʃi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗劣的，笨拙草率的</td><td>a botchy repair job<br>粗劣的修补活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The botchy paintwork peeled within a single winter.  粗劣的漆面一个冬天就剥落了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 messy</td><td>/ˈmesi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，脏乱的</td><td>a messy bedroom<br>凌乱的卧室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His room was so messy that the floor disappeared.  他房间乱得连地板都看不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slapdash</td><td>/ˈslæpdæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>马虎的</td><td>a slapdash job<br>马虎的活儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His report was so slapdash that the editor refused to publish it.  他的报告太马虎，编辑拒绝发表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sloppy</td><td>/ˈslɑːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的；混乱的</td><td>sloppy handwriting<br>潦草的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sloppy handwriting made the exam answers almost impossible to read.  他潦草的字迹让考卷答案几乎无法辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slovenly</td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untidy</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，凌乱的</td><td>an untidy desk<br>凌乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His untidy desk was buried under unopened letters.  他凌乱的书桌被未开封的信件埋住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fastidious</td><td>/fæˈstɪdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，极仔细的，追求完美的</td><td>fastidious about details<br>对细节极挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fastidious about details and rereads every line twice.  她对细节极为挑剔，每一行都要读两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meticulous</td><td>/məˈtɪkjələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一丝不苟的，极为细致的</td><td>meticulous attention to detail<br>对细节极为在意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restorer's meticulous attention to detail saved the fragile manuscript from further damage.  修复师一丝不苟地关注细节，使这份脆弱的手稿免于进一步损坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sloppy</b></td><td>/ˈslɑːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的；混乱的</td><td>sloppy handwriting<br>潦草的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sloppy handwriting made the exam answers almost impossible to read.  他潦草的字迹让考卷答案几乎无法辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blowsy</del></td><td>/ˈblaʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，粗俗肥胖的</td><td>a blowsy appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played a blowsy landlady in that old comedy.  她在那部老喜剧里演一个邋遢的女房东。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dowdy</td><td>/ˈdaʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，土气的</td><td>a dowdy old coat<br>土气的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to wear the dowdy uniform to the party.  她拒绝穿那件土气的制服去聚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 frowsy</del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slovenly</td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
