<tr><td>↳近 unkempt</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkempt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，蓬乱的</td><td>unkempt hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the interview with unkempt hair and a wrinkled shirt.  他去面试时头发蓬乱，衬衫也皱皱巴巴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untidy</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，凌乱的</td><td>an untidy desk<br>凌乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His untidy desk was buried under unopened letters.  他凌乱的书桌被未开封的信件埋住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chaotic</td><td>/keɪˈɑːtɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，杂乱无章的</td><td>a chaotic desk<br>杂乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office looked chaotic after the sudden move.  突然搬迁后办公室看上去一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disarranged</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪndʒd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，被弄乱的</td><td>disarranged papers<br>凌乱的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The burglars left every drawer disarranged.  窃贼把每个抽屉都弄得凌乱不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disarrayed</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，无秩序的</td><td>disarrayed troops<br>阵形混乱的部队</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retreating army was hopelessly disarrayed by nightfall.  撤退的军队到夜幕降临时已完全溃乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disheveled</td><td>/dɪˈʃevəld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，蓬乱的</td><td>disheveled hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked disheveled after sleeping in his clothes.  他穿着衣服睡过之后显得很凌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorderly</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，无秩序的</td><td>a disorderly crowd<br>混乱的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Books lay in disorderly piles across the whole floor.  书杂乱地堆在整个地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 Jumbled</td><td>/ˈdʒʌmbəld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，杂乱的</td><td>a jumbled pile of clothes<br>杂乱的一堆衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His notes were so jumbled that nobody could follow them.  他的笔记杂乱得没人看得懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 littered</td><td>/ˈlɪtərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>散乱的，满是垃圾的</td><td>a littered street<br>满是垃圾的街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The beach was littered with plastic bottles after the festival.  节庆过后海滩上散落着塑料瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rumpled</td><td>/ˈrʌmpəld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>起皱的，凌乱的</td><td>a rumpled shirt<br>起皱的衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a rumpled suit and unpolished shoes.  他穿着起皱的西装和没擦亮的鞋子来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tousled</td><td>/ˈtaʊzəld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（头发）蓬乱的</td><td>tousled hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy woke with tousled hair and sleepy eyes.  男孩醒来时头发蓬乱、双眼困倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumbled</td><td>/ˈtʌmbəld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凌乱的，弄乱的</td><td>a tumbled bed<br>凌乱的床铺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the tumbled sheets exactly as they were.  她把凌乱的床单原样留着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dapper</td><td>/ˈdæpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a dapper little man<br>衣着整洁的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dapper old gentleman in a gray suit greeted us at the door.  一位身穿灰色西装、衣冠整洁的老先生在门口迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dashing</td><td>/ˈdæʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣着讲究的，潇洒时髦的</td><td>a dashing young officer<br>潇洒的年轻军官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dashing young officer looked immaculate in his dress uniform.  那位潇洒的年轻军官穿着礼服显得一丝不乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spruce</td><td>/spruːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>整洁漂亮的；打扮整洁</td><td>look neat and spruce<br>显得整洁利落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old waiter always looked spruce despite his worn uniform.  尽管制服磨旧，这位老侍者总是显得整洁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bandbox</td><td>/ˈbændbɑːks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>光鲜整洁的；帽盒</td><td>a bandbox appearance<br>光鲜整洁的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He always looked as if he had stepped out of a bandbox.  他总是一身光鲜整洁的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crisp</td><td>/ˈkrɪsp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挺括整洁的；脆的</td><td>a crisp white shirt<br>挺括的白衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a crisp white shirt to the interview.  他穿着一件挺括的白衬衫去面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kempt</td><td>/kɛmpt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，梳理整齐的</td><td>a kempt appearance<br>整洁的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived kempt and calm despite the long flight.  尽管长途飞行，他到达时仍整洁镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neat</td><td>/ˈniːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，井然有序的</td><td>neat handwriting<br>整洁的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her neat handwriting made the notes easy to read.  她整洁的字迹让笔记很好读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 organized</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有条理的，有组织的</td><td>highly organized files<br>条理分明的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She keeps an organized notebook for every course she takes.  她为每门课都备有一本条理分明的笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shipshape</td><td>/ˈʃɪpʃeɪp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的</td><td>keep the office shipshape<br>让办公室井然有序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor kept his tiny cabin perfectly shipshape at all times.  老水手始终把他的小舱室保持得井然有序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 snug</td><td>/ˈsnʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧凑的；温暖舒适的</td><td>a snug little room<br>小巧温馨的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin was snug but far too small for four guests.  小屋很温馨，但对四位客人来说太小了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tidy</td><td>/ˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>整洁的；整理</td><td>tidy up the room<br>把房间整理干净</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please tidy up the room before the guests arrive.  请在客人到来前把房间整理干净。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trim</td><td>/ˈtrɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>修剪；削减</td><td>trim the hedge<br>修剪树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He trims the hedge every June before the neighbors complain.  他每年六月在邻居抱怨前修剪树篱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sloth</b></td><td>/sloʊθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怠惰，懒惰</td><td>a life of sloth<br>懒散的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of sloth had left him unfit for any demanding job.  多年懒散使他无法胜任任何要求高的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idleness</td><td>/ˈaɪdəlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>懒散，无所事事</td><td>days of idleness<br>无所事事的日子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of idleness left him restless and irritable.  数周的无所事事让他焦躁易怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indolence</td><td>/ˈɪndələns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懒惰，懒散</td><td>sink into indolence<br>陷入懒散</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His natural indolence kept him from finishing anything.  他天生的懒惰使他什么事都做不完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inertia</td><td>/ɪˈnɜːrʃə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>惰性，懒惰；惯性</td><td>bureaucratic inertia<br>官僚的惰性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bureaucratic inertia delayed the reform for another decade.  官僚惰性使这项改革又推迟了十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 languor</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懒惰；衰弱</td><td>summer languor<br>夏日的倦怠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy heat brought a languor that made work nearly impossible.  酷热带来一种倦怠，几乎使人无法工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laziness</td><td>/ˈleɪzinəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>懒惰，懒散</td><td>sheer laziness<br>纯粹的懒惰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blamed his poor grades on laziness rather than ability.  他把成绩差归咎于懒惰而不是能力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lethargy</td><td>/ˈleθərdʒi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无生气，倦怠，昏睡</td><td>shake off his lethargy<br>摆脱他的倦怠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk helped him shake off his afternoon lethargy.  短短的散步帮他摆脱了午后的倦怠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 shiftlessness</del></td><td>/ˈʃɪftləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懒散，无进取心</td><td>criticize his shiftlessness<br>批评他的懒散</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old farmer despised the shiftlessness of his youngest son.  这位老农鄙视小儿子的懒散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sluggishness</td><td>/ˈslʌɡɪʃnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迟缓，无精打采</td><td>morning sluggishness<br>早晨的迟缓无力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat explained the general sluggishness of the crew.  炎热解释了船员们普遍的无精打采。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torpor</td><td>/ˈtɔːrpər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣；（肉体或精神上的）迟钝，懒散</td><td>sink into torpor<br>陷入迟钝麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat sank the whole village into a heavy afternoon torpor.  暑热让整个村子陷入沉沉的午后麻木。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 assiduity</del></td><td>/ˌæsɪˈduːəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勤勉，刻苦</td><td>work with assiduity<br>勤勉地工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pursued the research with remarkable assiduity for years.  他多年以非凡的勤勉从事这项研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diligence</td><td>/ˈdɪlədʒəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>勤奋，勤勉</td><td>with great diligence<br>非常勤奋地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her diligence earned her a place at the top university.  她的勤奋为她赢得了顶尖大学的名额。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 industry</td><td>/ˈɪndəstri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勤劳，勤奋；工业</td><td>habits of industry<br>勤劳的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher praised the boy's industry rather than his talent.  老师赞扬那男孩的勤奋而非天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slouch</b></td><td>/slaʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行走</td><td>slouch down the street<br>沿街缓慢走着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted workers slouched slowly down the street toward the bus stop.  疲惫的工人沿街缓慢走向公交站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drag</td><td>/ˈdræɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>累赘，阻力；拖曳</td><td>a drag on growth<br>增长的阻力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 High debt became a drag on the country's economy.  高额债务成了该国经济的阻力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inch</td><td>/ɪntʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）慢慢移动</td><td>inch forward slowly<br>缓慢向前挪动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy truck had to inch forward slowly across the snowy bridge.  那辆重卡只能缓慢地挪过积雪覆盖的桥梁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 limp</td><td>/lɪmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、形容词</td><td>跛行，艰难缓慢地走；柔软的</td><td>limp toward the door<br>跛行走向门口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He limped slowly toward the door with his ankle wrapped in bandages.  他脚踝缠着绷带，一步一跛地慢慢走向门口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nose</td><td>/ˈnoʊz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>打探，探听；嗅</td><td>nose into private matters<br>打探私事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters nosed around the office for two days looking for leaks.  记者在办公室四处打探了两天，想找到泄密者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ooze</td><td>/ˈuːz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>渗出，慢慢流出</td><td>ooze out of the wound<br>从伤口渗出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick sap oozed slowly from the cut in the bark.  浓稠的树液从树皮的切口慢慢渗出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plod</td><td>/ˈplɑːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>沉重缓慢地走，苦干</td><td>plod through the mud<br>在泥地里沉重跋涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired horses plodded through the deep mud.  疲惫的马匹在深泥中沉重跋涉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poke</td><td>/ˈpoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行进，闲荡</td><td>poke along the road<br>沿路慢慢晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old truck poked along the highway at thirty miles an hour.  那辆旧卡车以每小时三十英里在公路上慢慢晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snail</td><td>/ˈsneɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>蜗牛；缓慢行进</td><td>move at a snail's pace<br>以蜗牛般的速度前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic snailed along the flooded avenue all morning.  整个上午车流在被水淹的大道上蜗行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fly</td><td>/ˈflaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>飞奔，疾行；飞</td><td>fly down the street<br>沿街飞奔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She flew down the corridor to catch the departing train.  她飞奔穿过走廊去赶将要开走的火车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 race</td><td>/ˈreɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>疾走，飞奔；比赛</td><td>race to the finish<br>冲向终点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raced across the field to warn the others.  他飞奔穿过田野去警告其他人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 speed</td><td>/ˈspiːd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>快速行进，疾驰</td><td>speed down the highway<br>在公路上疾驰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ambulance sped down the empty highway toward the hospital.  救护车沿着空旷的公路疾驰驶向医院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whiz</td><td>/ˈwɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>飞驰，呼啸而过</td><td>whiz past the window<br>飞驰过窗前</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Motorcycles whizzed past us on the narrow mountain road.  摩托车在狭窄的山路上从我们身旁飞驰而过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 zip</td><td>/ˈzɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>飞快移动；拉上拉链</td><td>zip past the window<br>飞快掠过窗前</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A motorcycle zipped past the window and vanished.  一辆摩托车飞快掠过窗前，消失不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slovenly</b></td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blowsy</del></td><td>/ˈblaʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，粗俗肥胖的</td><td>a blowsy appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played a blowsy landlady in that old comedy.  她在那部老喜剧里演一个邋遢的女房东。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dowdy</td><td>/ˈdaʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，土气的</td><td>a dowdy old coat<br>土气的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to wear the dowdy uniform to the party.  她拒绝穿那件土气的制服去聚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 frowsy</del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sloppy</td><td>/ˈslɑːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的；混乱的</td><td>sloppy handwriting<br>潦草的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sloppy handwriting made the exam answers almost impossible to read.  他潦草的字迹让考卷答案几乎无法辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unkempt</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkempt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，蓬乱的</td><td>unkempt hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the interview with unkempt hair and a wrinkled shirt.  他去面试时头发蓬乱，衬衫也皱皱巴巴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untidy</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，凌乱的</td><td>an untidy desk<br>凌乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His untidy desk was buried under unopened letters.  他凌乱的书桌被未开封的信件埋住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dapper</td><td>/ˈdæpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a dapper little man<br>衣着整洁的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dapper old gentleman in a gray suit greeted us at the door.  一位身穿灰色西装、衣冠整洁的老先生在门口迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dashing</td><td>/ˈdæʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣着讲究的，潇洒时髦的</td><td>a dashing young officer<br>潇洒的年轻军官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dashing young officer looked immaculate in his dress uniform.  那位潇洒的年轻军官穿着礼服显得一丝不乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neat</td><td>/ˈniːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，井然有序的</td><td>neat handwriting<br>整洁的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her neat handwriting made the notes easy to read.  她整洁的字迹让笔记很好读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spruce</td><td>/spruːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>整洁漂亮的；打扮整洁</td><td>look neat and spruce<br>显得整洁利落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old waiter always looked spruce despite his worn uniform.  尽管制服磨旧，这位老侍者总是显得整洁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trim</td><td>/ˈtrɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>修剪；削减</td><td>trim the hedge<br>修剪树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He trims the hedge every June before the neighbors complain.  他每年六月在邻居抱怨前修剪树篱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>sluggard</b></del></td><td>/ˈslʌɡərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懒人</td><td>a hopeless sluggard<br>无可救药的懒人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brother is a hopeless sluggard who sleeps until noon every day.  她弟弟是个无可救药的懒人，每天睡到中午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deadbeat</td><td>/ˈdedˌbiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懒汉，游手好闲者</td><td>a lazy deadbeat<br>懒散的游手好闲者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His landlord called him a deadbeat who never paid rent.  房东说他是个从不付租金的懒汉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drone</td><td>/droʊn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>低沉单调地说；嗡嗡地叫</td><td>drone on about details<br>唠唠叨叨讲细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker droned on for another twenty minutes.  演讲者又单调地讲了二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idler</td><td>/ˈaɪdlər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>懒人，闲荡者</td><td>an incorrigible idler<br>无可救药的懒人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foreman had no patience for idlers on his crew.  工头对手下的懒人毫无耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loafer</td><td>/ˈloʊfər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>游手好闲的人；便鞋</td><td>a shameless loafer<br>无耻的游手好闲者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man dismissed his nephew as a loafer.  老人把侄子看作一个游手好闲的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slouch</td><td>/slaʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行走</td><td>slouch down the street<br>沿街缓慢走着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted workers slouched slowly down the street toward the bus stop.  疲惫的工人沿街缓慢走向公交站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slug</td><td>/ˈslʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>懒虫；鼻涕虫</td><td>lie around like a slug<br>像懒虫一样躺着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lay on the sofa all weekend like a slug.  他整个周末像懒虫一样躺在沙发上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doer</td><td>/ˈduːər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>实干者，做事的人</td><td>a doer not a talker<br>实干者而非空谈者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company needed doers rather than eloquent planners.  公司需要的是实干者，而不是能言善辩的策划者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hummer</td><td>/ˈhʌmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精力充沛的人；哼唱者</td><td>a real hummer at work<br>工作上的干将</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their new manager is a hummer who never slows down.  他们的新经理是个从不放慢脚步的干将。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hustler</td><td>/ˈhʌslər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>干劲十足的人；骗子</td><td>a natural born hustler<br>天生的拼命干将</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a young salesman he was known as a hustler.  年轻时做销售，他以干劲十足闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rustler</td><td>/ˈrʌslər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精明能干的人；偷牲畜者</td><td>a tireless rustler<br>不倦的能干之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their new foreman is a rustler who never wastes a single minute.  他们的新工头是个从不浪费一分钟的能干之人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sluggish</b></td><td>/ˈslʌɡɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行动迟缓的，懒散的</td><td>feel sluggish all morning<br>整个上午都懒洋洋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt sluggish all morning and could barely finish his simplest chores.  他整个上午都懒洋洋的，连最简单的杂事都几乎做不完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crawling</td><td>/ˈkrɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢爬行的，龟速的</td><td>crawling traffic<br>龟速的车流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We sat in crawling traffic for nearly two hours.  我们在龟速车流中坐了近两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creeping</td><td>/ˈkriːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢移动的，蔓延的</td><td>a creeping pace<br>缓慢的速度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform advanced at a creeping pace for years.  这项改革多年来以极缓慢的速度推进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dallying</td><td>/ˈdæliɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲混的，磨蹭的</td><td>a dallying manner<br>磨蹭的样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dallying approach to deadlines infuriated the whole team.  他对截止日期磨蹭的态度让整个团队恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dilatory</td><td>/ˈdɪlətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖延的，磨蹭的</td><td>dilatory tactics<br>拖延的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dilatory response to the complaint annoyed every member of the committee.  他对投诉迟迟不作回应，令委员会每位成员恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dragging</td><td>/ˈdræɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖沓的，缓慢的</td><td>a dragging negotiation<br>拖沓的谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dragging trial exhausted witnesses on both sides.  拖沓的审判耗尽了双方证人的精力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lagging</td><td>/ˈlæɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>落后的，迟缓的</td><td>lagging sales figures<br>落后的销售数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers worried about the lagging performance of two branches.  经理们担心两家分店迟缓的业绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leisurely</td><td>/ˈliːʒərli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>缓慢的，从容不迫的</td><td>a leisurely walk<br>从容的散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They took a leisurely stroll along the riverbank after dinner.  晚饭后他们沿河岸悠闲地散步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 snaillike</del></td><td>/ˈsneɪllaɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢如蜗牛的</td><td>a snaillike pace<br>蜗牛般的速度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiations moved at a snaillike pace for months.  谈判数月来以蜗牛般的速度进行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tardy</td><td>/ˈtɑːrdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢的，迟缓的</td><td>a tardy response<br>迟缓的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's tardy response angered customers waiting for their refunds.  公司迟缓的回应让等退款的顾客很生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unhurried</td><td>/ʌnˈhɜːrid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>从容的，不慌忙的</td><td>an unhurried pace<br>从容的步调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He works in an unhurried way but never misses a deadline.  他做事从容，却从不误期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bolting</td><td>/ˈboʊltɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞奔的，突然疾冲的</td><td>a bolting horse<br>狂奔的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bolting horse dragged the cart across the yard.  一匹狂奔的马把车拖过了院子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brisk</td><td>/brɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满生机的，有活力的</td><td>a brisk walk<br>快步散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brisk walk before breakfast leaves me alert all morning.  早餐前快走一趟能让我整个上午都很清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fast</td><td>/fæst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快的</td><td>a fast runner<br>跑得快的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a fast worker who finishes every job ahead of schedule.  他做事很快，每项工作都提前完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fleet</td><td>/fliːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的</td><td>fleet of foot<br>脚步迅捷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fleet young messenger crossed the valley before dark.  这位迅捷的年轻信使天黑前穿过了山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hasty</td><td>/ˈheɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，仓促的；轻率的</td><td>a hasty judgment<br>轻率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hasty decision made in anger often costs far more later.  盛怒之下作出的轻率决定往往日后代价更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lightning</td><td>/ˈlaɪtnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>闪电般迅速的；闪电</td><td>at lightning speed<br>以闪电般的速度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The runner reacted with lightning speed to the starting gun.  这名选手对发令枪的反应快如闪电。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meteoric</td><td>/ˌmiːtiˈɔːrɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流星般迅速而短暂的</td><td>a meteoric rise<br>迅速而短暂的崛起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her meteoric rise in the company ended just as suddenly as it began.  她在公司里的迅速崛起结束得和开始时一样突然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quick</td><td>/ˈkwɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快的，迅速的</td><td>a quick decision<br>迅速的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A quick decision saved the patient's life that night.  一个迅速的决定在那晚救了病人的命。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 racing</td><td>/ˈreɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞驰的，急速的</td><td>a racing pulse<br>急速的脉搏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He woke with a racing heart and damp forehead.  他醒来时心跳飞快，额头潮湿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapid</td><td>/ˈræpəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快速的</td><td>rapid economic growth<br>迅速的经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city saw rapid change after the railway finally arrived.  铁路终于通达后这座城市迅速变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rocketing</td><td>/ˈrɑːkətɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞涨的，猛升的</td><td>rocketing housing prices<br>飞涨的房价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rocketing fuel costs forced the airline to cancel routes.  飞涨的燃油成本迫使航空公司取消航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 speedy</td><td>/ˈspiːdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快速的，迅速的</td><td>a speedy recovery<br>迅速康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone wished her a speedy recovery after the operation.  手术后大家都祝她迅速康复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swift</td><td>/ˈswɪft/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，敏捷的</td><td>a swift response<br>迅速的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her swift decision saved the patient a great deal of pain.  她迅速的决定让病人少受许多痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slumber</b></td><td>/ˈslʌmbər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>安睡，沉睡</td><td>fall into a deep slumber<br>陷入沉睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The baby slumbered peacefully while the storm raged outside the window.  窗外风雨交加，婴儿仍安然沉睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catnap</td><td>/ˈkætˌnæp/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>小睡，打盹</td><td>take a quick catnap<br>小睡一会儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took a short catnap before the evening shift.  她在晚班前小睡了一会儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doze</td><td>/doʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>小憩</td><td>doze off in class<br>上课打瞌睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dozed off in class and missed the announcement about the exam.  她上课打瞌睡，错过了关于考试的通知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nap</td><td>/ˈnæp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小睡，打盹</td><td>take a short nap<br>小睡一会儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He naps for twenty minutes after lunch every day.  他每天午饭后小睡二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rest</td><td>/ˈrest/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>休息，静止；剩余部分</td><td>take a short rest<br>稍作休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor ordered him to rest for at least a week.  医生要他至少休息一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sleep</td><td>/ˈsliːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>睡眠；睡觉</td><td>fall into a deep sleep<br>进入深睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fell into a deep sleep the moment he lay down.  他一躺下就进入了深睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snooze</td><td>/ˈsnuːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小睡，打瞌睡</td><td>have a quick snooze<br>打个短盹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Grandfather likes a snooze in the armchair after lunch.  祖父喜欢午饭后在扶手椅上打个盹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arouse</td><td>/əˈraʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，激起；唤醒</td><td>arouse public suspicion<br>引起公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing files aroused suspicion among the auditors.  丢失的文件引起了审计人员的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 awake</td><td>/əˈweɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>醒着的；唤醒</td><td>lie awake at night<br>夜里醒着躺着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise from the street kept me awake until dawn.  街上的噪音让我一直醒到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waken</td><td>/ˈweɪkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>唤醒，醒来</td><td>waken the sleeping child<br>唤醒睡着的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try not to waken the baby when you come in.  你进来时尽量不要弄醒宝宝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>slur</b></td><td>/slɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说；诋毁，污辱</td><td>slur one's words<br>说话含糊不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so tired that he began to slur his words.  他累得开始说话含糊不清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blot</td><td>/ˈblɑːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，瑕疵；污渍</td><td>a blot on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That scandal remains a blot on the family name.  那件丑闻至今是这个家族名声上的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brand</td><td>/ˈbrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污名，耻辱印记；品牌</td><td>the brand of disgrace<br>耻辱的印记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict left a brand of shame on his reputation.  这个判决给他的名声留下了耻辱印记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 onus</td><td>/ˈoʊnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>责任，负担；污名</td><td>the onus of proof<br>举证的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The onus of proving the claim falls on the plaintiff.  证明该主张的责任落在原告身上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 smirch</del></td><td>/smɜrtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>玷污（名誉）</td><td>smirch someone's reputation<br>玷污某人名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rumor smirched the reputation he had built over thirty years.  谣言玷污了他三十年建立起来的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smudge</td><td>/ˈsmʌdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污迹，污点；弄脏</td><td>a smudge of ink<br>一处墨迹污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A smudge of ink spoiled the otherwise perfect page.  一处墨迹污点毁了这本来完美的一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stigma</td><td>/ˈstɪɡmə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>耻辱，污名</td><td>the stigma of failure<br>失败带来的污名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stigma of failure kept him from applying for another job.  失败带来的污名使他不敢再去申请工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taint</td><td>/teɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使（品质）污损</td><td>taint someone's reputation<br>损害某人名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal tainted his reputation for the rest of his career.  这场丑闻在他余下的职业生涯中都损害着他的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bypass</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌpæs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>绕过，避开</td><td>bypass the rules<br>绕开规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new road bypasses the crowded center of the town.  新路绕开了拥挤的镇中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forget</td><td>/fərˈɡet/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忘记，忽略</td><td>forget the insult<br>忘掉那句侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He chose to forget the insult rather than answer it.  他选择忘掉那句侮辱，而不去回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overpass</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌpæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>忽略，越过</td><td>overpass minor faults<br>忽略小的过失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor overpassed several errors in the final draft.  编辑忽略了终稿中的几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 award</td><td>/əˈwɔːrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>授予，颁发；奖励</td><td>award a medal<br>颁发奖章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee awarded her a medal for outstanding service.  委员会因她的杰出贡献授予她一枚奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 credit</td><td>/ˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>表扬，赞扬；信任</td><td>take credit for<br>把功劳归于自己</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deserves credit for keeping the project alive.  她值得称赞，因为她让这个项目坚持下来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glory</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>感到自豪，得意；荣耀</td><td>glory in her success<br>为她的成功而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gloried in the praise his research finally received.  他为研究终于获得的赞誉而洋洋自得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heed</td><td>/ˈhiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>注意，留心</td><td>heed the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few sailors heeded the warning about the coming storm.  很少有水手留意关于风暴来临的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mind</td><td>/ˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>留意，注意；介意</td><td>mind the step<br>留意台阶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please mind the gap between the train and the platform.  请注意列车与站台之间的空隙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sly</b></td><td>/slaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a sly grin<br>狡黠的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sly grin spread across his face as he hid the letter.  他藏起那封信时，脸上露出狡黠的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguiling</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的；有欺骗性的</td><td>a beguiling but false promise<br>诱人却虚假的承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His beguiling manner hid a thoroughly calculating and selfish nature.  他迷人的举止掩盖了极其精于算计的自私本性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cagey</td><td>/ˈkeɪdʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不乐意说话的</td><td>cagey about the details<br>对细节含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was cagey about the reasons for her sudden resignation.  经理对她突然辞职的原因含糊其辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crafty</td><td>/ˈkræfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的，诡计多端的</td><td>a crafty politician<br>狡猾的政客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A crafty lawyer found a loophole nobody else had noticed.  一位狡猾的律师找到了别人都没注意的漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cunning</td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a cunning scheme<br>狡猾的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fox was cunning enough to avoid every trap they set.  这只狐狸够狡猾，避开了他们设的每个陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 designing</td><td>/dɪˈzaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有阴谋的，狡诈的</td><td>a designing rival<br>工于心计的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She soon saw through her designing business partner.  她很快看透了那个工于心计的生意伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foxy</td><td>/ˈfɑːksi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的，机敏的</td><td>a foxy old trader<br>狡猾的老商人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foxy old trader always knew when to sell everything.  这位狡猾的老商人总知道何时全部卖出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 guileful</del></td><td>/ˈɡaɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，欺骗的</td><td>a guileful negotiator<br>狡诈的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guileful merchant tricked several villagers out of their savings.  这个狡诈的商人骗走了几位村民的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scheming</td><td>/ˈskiːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>搞阴谋的，诡计多端的</td><td>a scheming courtier<br>诡计多端的臣子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play centers on a scheming courtier and a weak king.  这部剧围绕一个诡计多端的臣子和一位软弱的国王展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrewd</td><td>/ʃruːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精明的，机敏的</td><td>a shrewd judgment<br>精明的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a shrewd judgment that saved the company millions of dollars.  那是个精明的判断，为公司省下了数百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subtle</td><td>/ˈsʌtl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，难以感知的</td><td>a subtle difference<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a subtle difference between these two shades of blue.  这两种蓝色之间有细微的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tricky</td><td>/ˈtrɪki/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的；棘手的</td><td>a tricky opponent<br>难对付的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parking here is tricky, especially during the busy evening hours.  这里停车很棘手，尤其在傍晚的繁忙时段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wily</td><td>/ˈwaɪli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，狡猾的</td><td>a wily negotiator<br>狡猾的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wily negotiator, he never revealed his lowest acceptable price.  作为狡猾的谈判者，他从不透露自己能接受的最低价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guileless</td><td>/ˈɡaɪlləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不狡诈的</td><td>a guileless smile<br>天真无邪的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her guileless smile made everyone in the room trust her immediately.  她天真无邪的笑容让屋里所有人立刻信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ingenuous</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真淳朴的</td><td>an ingenuous young student<br>天真淳朴的年轻学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuous questions about the city amused the veteran taxi driver greatly.  他关于这座城市的天真提问让老出租车司机大为好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undesigning</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈzaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无心机的，坦率的</td><td>an undesigning honest remark<br>毫无心机的诚实话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undesigning honesty won friends wherever he happened to travel.  他毫无心机的诚实让他无论走到哪里都能交到朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smarmy</b></td><td>/ˈsmɑːrmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚情假意的，过分恭维的</td><td>a smarmy compliment<br>虚情假意的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman's smarmy praise made her uncomfortable.  推销员虚情假意的夸奖让她很不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fulsome</td><td>/ˈfʊlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，令人反感的</td><td>fulsome praise<br>过分的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fulsome praise embarrassed everyone in the room.  他过分的赞美让屋里每个人尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oily</td><td>/ˈɔɪli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>油滑的，谄媚的；油质的</td><td>an oily salesman<br>油嘴滑舌的推销员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His oily compliments made everyone in the room uncomfortable.  他油滑的奉承让屋里每个人都不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slick</td><td>/ˈslɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使光滑；光滑的</td><td>slick back one's hair<br>把头发抹平贴后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slicked back his hair before the interview.  面试前他把头发抹平贴后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soapy</td><td>/ˈsoʊpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谄媚的；含皂的</td><td>a soapy compliment<br>谄媚的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His soapy manner with the boss embarrassed his colleagues.  他对老板谄媚的样子让同事们难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unctuous</td><td>/ˈʌŋktʃuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油嘴滑舌的，谄媚的</td><td>an unctuous compliment<br>油腻的奉承</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unctuous salesman praised every choice we made in his store.  那个油嘴滑舌的销售员对我们在他店里的每个选择都大加赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 earnest</td><td>/ˈɜːrnɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃认真的</td><td>an earnest student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An earnest student stayed behind to ask about the summer reading list.  一名认真的学生留下来询问暑期阅读书目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heartfelt</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtfelt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真心实意的</td><td>heartfelt thanks<br>真诚的感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered heartfelt thanks to everyone who had supported his family.  他向所有支持过他家人的人表达了真心的感谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sincere</td><td>/sɪnˈsɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，诚挚的</td><td>a sincere apology<br>真诚的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sincere apology repaired a friendship damaged for years.  她真诚的道歉修复了受损多年的友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unaffected</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受影响的</td><td>unaffected by the crisis<br>未受危机影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Northern farms remained largely unaffected by the drought.  北方的农场基本未受这场旱灾影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>skullduggery</b></td><td>/skʌlˈdʌɡəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欺诈，诡计</td><td>political skullduggery<br>政治上的诡计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of the political skullduggery behind every appointment.  选民厌倦了每次任命背后的政治诡计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artifice</td><td>/ˈɑːrtəfɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计，欺骗；巧妙手段</td><td>use clever artifice<br>使用巧妙的诡计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her apparent shyness was pure artifice designed to disarm her rivals.  她表面的羞怯纯属伪装，为的是麻痹对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chicanery</td><td>/ʃɪˈkeɪnəri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计多端，欺骗</td><td>political chicanery<br>政治骗术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The election was decided by chicanery rather than by honest debate.  这次选举靠的是欺骗手段而非诚实辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trickery</td><td>/ˈtrɪkəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欺骗，诡计</td><td>resort to trickery<br>使用诡计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He won the contract through outright trickery.  他完全靠欺诈手段拿下了这份合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subterfuge</td><td>/ˈsʌbtərfjuːdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计</td><td>resort to subterfuge<br>采用诡计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The spy resorted to subterfuge to enter the heavily guarded building.  间谍采用诡计进入戒备森严的大楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wile</td><td>/ˈwaɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计，花招</td><td>use every wile<br>使尽各种花招</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She used every wile she knew to win his support.  她用尽一切手段来赢得他的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smattering</b></td><td>/ˈsmætərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量</td><td>a smattering of Spanish<br>略懂一点西班牙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He picked up a smattering of Spanish during his summer abroad.  他在国外过暑假时学了一点西班牙语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ace</td><td>/ˈeɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>高手，能人</td><td>an ace at chess<br>国际象棋高手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is an ace with numbers and finished the audit alone.  他是数字高手，独自完成了审计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bit</td><td>/ˈbɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一点，少量</td><td>a bit of luck<br>一点运气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Add just a bit of salt to the simmering sauce.  往煮着的酱汁里加一点点盐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 driblet</del></td><td>/ˈdrɪblət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，点滴</td><td>in driblets<br>一点一点地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 News of the accident arrived in driblets all evening.  事故的消息整晚一点一点地传来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glimmer</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一丝；微光</td><td>a glimmer of hope<br>一丝希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter offered a faint glimmer of hope.  那封信带来一丝微弱的希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handful</td><td>/ˈhændˌfʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少数，一把</td><td>only a handful of guests<br>只有少数客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a handful of guests stayed until the end.  只有少数客人留到最后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hint</td><td>/ˈhɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暗示，提示</td><td>drop a hint<br>透露一点暗示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave me a subtle hint about the surprise party.  她微妙地暗示了那个惊喜派对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mite</td><td>/maɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微小的东西，很少的钱</td><td>a mite of comfort<br>一点点安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brief letter offered a mite of comfort during those difficult winter months.  他简短的信在那些艰难的冬月里带来了一点点安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mouthful</td><td>/ˈmaʊθˌfʊl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一口；一大堆话</td><td>a mouthful of water<br>一口水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swallowed a mouthful of cold water and kept running.  他喝了一口凉水，继续奔跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nip</td><td>/nɪp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小口吃喝</td><td>a nip of whisky<br>一小口威士忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He poured himself a nip of whisky and sat down by the fire.  他给自己倒了一小口威士忌，坐到火边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ounce</td><td>/ˈaʊns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一点；盎司</td><td>every ounce of strength<br>每一分力气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used every ounce of strength to lift the beam.  他用尽每一分力气抬起那根梁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pittance</td><td>/ˈpɪtns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量津贴</td><td>work for a pittance<br>为微薄报酬工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The apprentices worked long hours for a pittance and no benefits.  学徒们长时间工作，只拿微薄津贴且没有福利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sprinkle</td><td>/ˈsprɪŋkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>少量，星星点点</td><td>a sprinkle of sugar<br>少量的糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cook added only a sprinkle of sugar to the batter.  厨师只往面糊里加了少量的糖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trace</td><td>/ˈtreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痕迹；微量</td><td>a trace of irony<br>一丝讽意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tea showed only a trace of bitterness.  这茶只有一丝苦味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered an abundance of fresh fruit at low prices.  市场以低价供应大量新鲜水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bundle</td><td>/ˈbʌndəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>匆忙送走，急送</td><td>bundle him into a car<br>把他匆匆塞进车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards bundled the prisoner into a waiting van.  卫兵把犯人匆匆塞进等候的货车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dozen</td><td>/ˈdʌzən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>许多，一打</td><td>dozens of complaints<br>数十起投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dozens of complaints arrived within the first week.  第一周内就来了数十起投诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 multiplicity</td><td>/ˌmʌltəˈplɪsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>多样性，多重性</td><td>a multiplicity of causes<br>多重原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failure resulted from a multiplicity of small errors.  这次失败源于多种小错误的叠加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 myriad</td><td>/ˈmɪriəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的，大量的</td><td>myriad possibilities<br>无数的可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new technology opens myriad possibilities for teaching students in remote villages.  这项新技术为教偏远村庄的学生打开了无数可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 plentitude</del></td><td>/ˈplɛnɪtud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，大量</td><td>a plentitude of talent<br>人才济济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The region enjoys a plentitude of water and fertile soil.  该地区拥有充足的水源和肥沃的土壤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 profusion</td><td>/prəˈfjuːʒn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>a profusion of flowers<br>繁花盛开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A profusion of wildflowers covered the hillside after the spring rains.  春雨过后，山坡上开满了大量野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scad</td><td>/ˈskæd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，许多</td><td>scads of money<br>大量的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He earned scads of money from that single invention.  他靠那一项发明赚了大量的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shipload</td><td>/ˈʃɪˌploʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一船的量；大量</td><td>a shipload of grain<br>一船谷物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shipload of grain reached the harbor before dawn.  黎明前一船谷物运抵港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slew</td><td>/sluː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，许多</td><td>a slew of problems<br>一大堆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new policy generated a slew of complaints from local business owners.  新政策引来当地商户的一大堆抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 volume</td><td>/ˈvɑːljum/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量；容量；卷</td><td>a great volume of traffic<br>巨大的车流量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge cannot handle this volume of traffic.  这座桥承受不了如此大的车流量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wealth</td><td>/ˈwelθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，丰富；财富</td><td>a wealth of experience<br>丰富的经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor brought a wealth of experience to the team.  这位老编辑给团队带来了丰富的经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smirk</b></td><td>/smɜːrk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（自鸣得意地）笑</td><td>smirk at the news<br>听到消息得意地笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy smirked when he heard that his rival had failed.  听说对手失败了，男孩得意地笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grin</td><td>/ɡrɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>咧嘴笑，咧嘴笑着表示</td><td>grin from ear to ear<br>笑得合不上嘴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grinned broadly when the judges announced his name as the winner.  当评委宣布他获胜时，他开心地咧嘴大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simper</td><td>/ˈsɪmpər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>傻笑，作态地笑</td><td>simper at the compliment<br>被夸时傻笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She simpered whenever the young officer looked her way.  每当那位年轻军官看向她，她就傻笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cry</td><td>/ˈkraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>呼喊，恳求；哭</td><td>a cry for help<br>求助的呼声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers sent out a desperate cry for outside help.  村民们发出绝望的呼声请求外界援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weep</td><td>/ˈwiːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>哭泣，流泪</td><td>weep bitterly<br>痛哭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some viewers still weep at the closing scene of that old film.  有些观众看到那部老电影的结尾时依然会哭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smother</b></td><td>/ˈsmʌðər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制（表达、说出），压制</td><td>smother a laugh<br>忍住不笑出声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned away to smother a laugh at her odd remark.  他转过身去，忍住对她那句怪话的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bridle</td><td>/ˈbraɪdl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>限制</td><td>bridle his anger<br>克制他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bridled her temper and answered the rude question politely.  她克制住脾气，礼貌地回答了那个无礼的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 choke</td><td>/ˈtʃoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使窒息；压制</td><td>choke on the smoke<br>被烟呛住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick smoke nearly choked the firefighters in the hallway.  浓烟几乎使走廊里的消防员窒息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 control</td><td>/kənˈtroʊl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>控制权，支配；控制</td><td>seize control of<br>夺取…的控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebels seized control of the capital within a single night.  叛军一夜之间就夺取了首都的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curb</td><td>/ˈkɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>控制，约束</td><td>curb government spending<br>控制政府开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city introduced new rules to curb traffic downtown.  市政府出台新规以限制市区交通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muffle</td><td>/ˈmʌfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压低声音，包裹使消声</td><td>muffle the sound<br>把声音压低</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick curtains muffle the sound of traffic outside the window.  厚窗帘减弱了窗外的车流声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quash</td><td>/kwɔːʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除；镇压，阻止</td><td>quash a conviction<br>撤销有罪判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeal court quashed his conviction last week.  上诉法院上周撤销了对他的定罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quell</td><td>/kwel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制；使平静，使安静</td><td>quell the unrest<br>平息动乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops were sent in to quell the growing unrest.  部队被派去平息日益扩大的动乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repress</td><td>/rɪˈpres/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>镇压；阻止（正常的表达、活动或发展等）</td><td>repress a smile<br>忍住笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to repress a smile during the solemn ceremony.  在庄严的仪式上她努力忍住笑意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squelch</td><td>/skweltʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制，镇压（运动）</td><td>squelch the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops moved in quickly to squelch the rebellion in the province.  军队迅速开进，镇压该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suppress</td><td>/səˈpres/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用暴力终止，镇压；封锁，删除</td><td>suppress a rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime used the army to suppress a rebellion in the capital.  该政权动用军队镇压首都的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unleash</td><td>/ənˈliːʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放，发动</td><td>unleash a storm of protest<br>引发一场抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision unleashed criticism from every corner of the press.  这一决定引发了媒体各方的批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vent</td><td>/ˈvent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排放，发泄；通风口</td><td>vent one's frustration<br>发泄挫败感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She vented her frustration in a long letter she never mailed.  她在一封从未寄出的长信里发泄了挫败感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smug</b></td><td>/smʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自大的，自鸣得意的</td><td>a smug expression<br>自鸣得意的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a smug expression after winning the argument.  争论获胜后他一脸自鸣得意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complacent</td><td>/kəmˈpleɪsənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自满的，自鸣得意的</td><td>grow complacent about safety<br>对安全变得自满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team grew complacent about safety after years without accidents.  多年没有事故后，团队对安全变得自满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 egoistic</td><td>/ˌiːɡoʊˈɪstɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>利己的，以自我为中心的</td><td>egoistic motives behind it<br>其背后的利己动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics detected egoistic motives behind his sudden generosity toward the museum.  批评者察觉到他突然对博物馆慷慨背后的利己动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proud</td><td>/ˈpraʊd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自豪的；傲慢的</td><td>too proud to ask<br>自傲得不肯求人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too proud to admit that he needed help.  他太自傲，不肯承认自己需要帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-satisfied</td><td>/ˌsɛlf ˈsætɪsfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自满的，自鸣得意的</td><td>a self-satisfied smile<br>自满的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His self-satisfied smile irritated everyone at the table.  他自鸣得意的笑让桌边所有人都不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vain</td><td>/ˈveɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒劳的；自负的</td><td>in vain<br>徒劳地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They searched all night in vain for the missing dog.  他们整夜寻找失踪的狗，却是徒劳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 egoless</del></td><td>/ˈiːɡoʊləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无自我的，不自我中心的</td><td>an egoless leader<br>没有自我的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teamwork requires an almost egoless attitude from everyone.  良好的团队合作要求每个人几乎都放下自我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>smuggling</b></td><td>/ˈsmʌɡlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>走私，私运</td><td>arrested for smuggling drugs<br>因走私毒品被捕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two sailors were arrested for smuggling diamonds through the busy port.  两名水手因通过繁忙港口走私钻石被捕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bootlegging</td><td>/ˈbuːtˌleɡɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>私酒买卖，非法贩运</td><td>bootlegging during Prohibition<br>禁酒时期的私酒贩运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a fortune from bootlegging during the 1920s.  他在二十年代靠贩运私酒发了财。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contraband</td><td>/ˈkɑːntrəˌbænd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>走私货；违禁品</td><td>seize the contraband<br>查获走私货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customs officers seized the contraband hidden in the crates.  海关人员查获了藏在板条箱里的走私货。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>snare</b></td><td>/sner/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>陷阱，罗网；用陷阱捕捉</td><td>snare a rabbit<br>用套索捕兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hunter set a snare near the stream to catch rabbits.  猎人在溪边设下套索捕兔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entanglement</td><td>/enˈtæŋɡəlmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纠缠，牵连</td><td>a legal entanglement<br>法律上的纠缠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years escaping a costly legal entanglement.  他花了数年才摆脱一场昂贵的法律纠缠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mesh</td><td>/meʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱捕</td><td>mesh the prey<br>捕获猎物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dolphins are sometimes meshed in nets meant for other fish.  海豚有时会被捕捉其他鱼类的渔网缠住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 morass</td><td>/məˈræs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，泥沼</td><td>a morass of debt<br>债务的泥沼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sank into a morass of legal disputes.  公司陷入了法律纠纷的泥沼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 net</td><td>/ˈnet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>用网捕捉，抓住</td><td>net a butterfly<br>网住一只蝴蝶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The police netted the thief before he could reach the border.  警察在小偷逃到边境前将他抓获。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noose</td><td>/ˈnuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>活套索，绞索</td><td>tighten the noose<br>收紧绞索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators slowly tightened the noose around the smuggling ring.  调查人员慢慢收紧了对走私团伙的包围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quagmire</td><td>/ˈkwæɡmaɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，泥潭</td><td>a legal quagmire<br>法律上的泥潭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company slowly sank into a quagmire of lawsuits.  公司慢慢陷入了诉讼的泥潭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quicksand</td><td>/ˈkwɪkˌsænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流沙；难以摆脱的困境</td><td>sink into quicksand<br>陷入流沙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing more money each month felt like stepping into quicksand.  每月借更多的钱，感觉就像踏入流沙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toil</td><td>/ˈtɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>辛苦劳作，苦干</td><td>toil in the fields<br>在田里劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers toiled from sunrise until dark during harvest.  收获时农民从日出劳作到天黑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trap</td><td>/ˈtræp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>困住，使陷入；陷阱</td><td>trap him in a lie<br>使他陷入自己的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow trapped the climbers on the ridge for days.  大雪把登山者困在山脊上好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catch</td><td>/ˈkætʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>染上（疾病）；抓住</td><td>catch a cold<br>感冒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You will catch a cold if you go out with wet hair.  你要是湿着头发出门就会感冒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enmesh</del></td><td>/ɪnˈmeʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使卷入，使陷入</td><td>enmesh in a scandal<br>使卷入丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became enmeshed in a lawsuit that lasted a decade.  他被卷入一场持续十年的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ensnare</td><td>/ɪnˈsner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱捕，使落入陷阱</td><td>ensnare a wild rabbit<br>捕住一只野兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap loans ensnared many families in years of debt.  廉价贷款使许多家庭陷入多年债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ensnarl</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsnɑːrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使纠缠，使陷入混乱</td><td>ensnarl the traffic downtown<br>使市中心交通陷入混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fallen crane ensnarled traffic for the entire morning.  倒下的吊车使整个上午的交通陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrap</td><td>/ɪnˈtræp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>entrap an unwary buyer<br>诱骗不留神的买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The con artist entrapped an unwary buyer with a forged certificate.  骗子用一张伪造证书诱骗了一位不留神的买主。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tangle</td><td>/ˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>纠缠，使…纠结</td><td>tangle the fishing line<br>把鱼线弄缠住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wind tangled the fishing line around the branches overhead.  风把鱼线缠在头顶的树枝上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disentangle</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，理清，使脱离</td><td>disentangle the wires<br>把电线理清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disentangle fact from rumor in the report.  在这份报告中把事实与谣言分开花了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>snarl</b></td><td>/snɑːrl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>纠缠，纠结</td><td>snarled traffic<br>堵成一团的车流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow snarled traffic across the city for most of the morning.  大雪使全城交通整个上午陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entangle</td><td>/ɪnˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使缠住，使卷入；使复杂</td><td>entangle the negotiations<br>使谈判变复杂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New objections entangled the negotiations and delayed the deal for weeks.  新的反对意见使谈判陷入纠缠，把交易推迟了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ensnarl</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsnɑːrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使纠缠，使陷入混乱</td><td>ensnarl the traffic downtown<br>使市中心交通陷入混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fallen crane ensnarled traffic for the entire morning.  倒下的吊车使整个上午的交通陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interlace</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈleɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>交织，缠结在一起</td><td>interlace the branches<br>把枝条交织起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The willow branches interlaced above the narrow path.  柳枝在窄径上方交织在一起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intertwine</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈtwaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>缠绕，交织</td><td>intertwine their fingers<br>十指相扣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their lives intertwined after that chance meeting in Rome.  在罗马那次偶遇后，他们的生活便交织在一起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 intertwist</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈtwɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>缠绕在一起，扭结</td><td>intertwist the ropes<br>把绳子扭结起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vines intertwist so tightly that they cannot be separated.  这些藤蔓缠绕得太紧，无法分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interweave</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈwiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>交织，编织在一起</td><td>interweave two themes<br>把两个主题交织</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel interweaves two family histories across a century.  这部小说把跨越一个世纪的两个家族史交织起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knot</td><td>/ˈnɑːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>结；纽带；打结</td><td>tie a knot<br>打一个结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sailor tied a knot that no amount of pulling could loosen.  水手打了个怎么拉都松不开的结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ravel</td><td>/ˈrævl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，松开；阐明</td><td>ravel out the thread<br>把线头解开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She patiently began to ravel out the tangled thread with a needle.  她耐心地用针把缠住的线头解开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tangle</td><td>/ˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>纠缠，使…纠结</td><td>tangle the fishing line<br>把鱼线弄缠住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wind tangled the fishing line around the branches overhead.  风把鱼线缠在头顶的树枝上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disentangle</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，理清，使脱离</td><td>disentangle the wires<br>把电线理清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disentangle fact from rumor in the report.  在这份报告中把事实与谣言分开花了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extricate</td><td>/ˈekstrɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使解脱，救出</td><td>extricate himself from debt<br>使自己脱离债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him years to extricate himself from serious debt.  他花了好几年才使自己脱离沉重债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unravel</td><td>/ənˈrævəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解开，拆散；弄清，解决</td><td>unravel the mystery<br>解开谜团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Detectives worked for months to unravel the tangled web of lies.  侦探们花了几个月才解开这张谎言之网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unsnarl</del></td><td>/ʌnˈsnɑrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开缠结，理清</td><td>unsnarl the tangled wires<br>理清缠住的电线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took an hour to unsnarl the fishing line.  理清那根钓鱼线花了一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 untwine</del></td><td>/ʌnˈtwaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开缠绕，松开</td><td>untwine the two ropes<br>把两根绳解开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He patiently untwined the vine from the iron gate.  他耐心地把藤蔓从铁门上解下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untwist</td><td>/ʌnˈtwɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>解开，使不再缠结</td><td>untwist the tangled wire<br>解开缠结的电线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She patiently untwisted the tangled headphone cable.  她耐心地解开缠成一团的耳机线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sneer</b></td><td>/snɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（轻蔑地）嘲笑</td><td>sneer at his efforts<br>嘲笑他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics sneered at his efforts, but he kept working on the design.  批评者嘲笑他的努力，他却继续完善设计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deride</td><td>/dɪˈraɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄，嘲笑</td><td>deride the proposal<br>嘲笑该提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics derided the proposal as naive and completely out of touch.  批评者嘲笑该提案幼稚且完全脱离现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gibe</td><td>/dʒaɪb/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>嘲弄</td><td>gibe at his accent<br>嘲弄他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The other students gibed at his accent until he stopped speaking.  其他学生嘲弄他的口音，直到他不再开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laugh</td><td>/ˈlæf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑；笑</td><td>laugh at his accent<br>嘲笑他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The other boys laughed at his worn-out shoes.  别的男孩嘲笑他那双破旧的鞋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeer</td><td>/ˈdʒɪr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑，讥讽</td><td>jeer at the loser<br>嘲笑失败者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd jeered at the player who missed the penalty.  人群嘲笑那名罚失点球的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jibe</td><td>/dʒaɪb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>意见一致</td><td>jibe with the facts<br>与事实相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account of the accident does not jibe with the physical evidence.  他对这场事故的说法与实物证据不符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ridicule</td><td>/ˈrɪdɪkjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑；讥讽；愚弄</td><td>an object of ridicule<br>被嘲笑的对象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Classmates began to ridicule him for his old shoes and worn coat.  同学们开始因他的旧鞋和破外套而嘲笑他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revere</td><td>/rɪˈvɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（尤指对神的）尊崇，尊敬</td><td>revered as a hero<br>被尊崇为英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students in the village still revere the teacher who founded their school.  村里的学生仍然尊崇创办他们学校的那位老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerate</td><td>/ˈvenəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>尊敬</td><td>venerate their ancestors<br>崇敬他们的祖先</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers still venerate their ancestors with offerings twice each year.  村民每年两次以供品崇敬他们的祖先。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>snobbish</b></td><td>/ˈsnɑːbɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>势利的，谄上傲下的</td><td>a snobbish attitude<br>势利的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her snobbish remarks about the neighbors offended everyone.  她对邻居的势利言论让所有人不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aristocratic</td><td>/əˌrɪstəˈkrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贵族的，高贵的</td><td>aristocratic manners<br>贵族式的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His aristocratic manners made the other guests uneasy.  他贵族式的举止让其他客人不太自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elitist</td><td>/eɪˈliːtɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>精英主义的；精英主义者</td><td>an elitist attitude<br>精英主义的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the admissions policy narrow and elitist.  批评者称这项招生政策狭隘且精英主义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persnickety</td><td>/pərˈsnɪkəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分挑剔的</td><td>a persnickety inspector<br>过分挑剔的检查员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The persnickety inspector rejected the wall over one small crack.  这位挑剔的检查员因一条小裂缝就否决了那面墙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 egoless</del></td><td>/ˈiːɡoʊləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无自我的，不自我中心的</td><td>an egoless leader<br>没有自我的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teamwork requires an almost egoless attitude from everyone.  良好的团队合作要求每个人几乎都放下自我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>snub</b></td><td>/snʌb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，不理睬</td><td>deliberately snub a colleague<br>故意冷落同事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deliberately snubbed her colleague by ignoring him at the meeting.  她在会上故意不理睬同事，冷落了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contemn</del></td><td>/kənˈtem/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，蔑视</td><td>contemn the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed to contemn every tradition the school held dear.  他似乎蔑视学校珍视的每一项传统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disdain</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻视，鄙视</td><td>disdain to answer<br>不屑于回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She disdained the flattery of her ambitious colleagues.  她鄙视那些野心勃勃的同事的奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disrespect</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>轻蔑，不敬；不尊重</td><td>show disrespect for elders<br>对长辈不敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Interrupting her that way showed real disrespect.  那样打断她实在很不尊重人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 look down</td><td>/ˈlʊk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>轻视，看不起</td><td>look down on him<br>看不起他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She never looked down on people with less education.  她从不看不起受教育较少的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
